# Changelog

## Unreleased

> Evidence correction (2026-11-04): prior E1 closure entries below retain historical readings. [The current audit](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#g5-e1-evidence-repair) supersedes the poseLocal-last-writer/completion, worker-pool, per-frame timing and uninterrupted-zero-flag interpretations. Source sampling-entry liveness remains observed; full E1 completion/lifetime/coherent-snapshot evidence remains pending. The current repair is not deployed or game-validated.

> Research-status clarification (2026-10-26): dated experiment entries below retain their original implementation plans and then-current hypotheses. [Current G1/G2 evidence](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#current-status) supersedes stale “awaiting run”, complete-isolation and single-root-cause interpretations; original contracts/logs remain intact. This clarification changes documentation only.

> Posterior clarification (2026-10-27): the dated audit18–21 entries retain their original interpretations. Their unique-generation and manager-registration necessity claims are superseded by [audit22](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit22); audit21's scratch-shell behavior guard remains valid. [Audit23](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit23) adds complete chained-unwind ranges and separates poseLocal copies from outer scene application. These static corrections do not establish a runtime pass.

### Added

- Implement [C-G5-E4](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e4) as v89 (2026-11-10, **built, not deployed**): the source capture lives inside `SourceTimeline` and adds **no detour**, so the instruments-do-not-stack rule stays intact and `poseLocal` is read after the frame's apply as a completed output. Poses come from `hkbCharacter::poseLocal`/`numPoseLocal`; the clip table walks the active-node list and keeps only `hkbClipGenerator` entries; the quad components are `memcpy`-ed because `hkVector4`/`hkQuaternion` have no named members — the first build failed on exactly that and the fix is recorded. Anchoring is one snapshot per settle: several variables settling on one frame count once, a settle arriving while an anchor is pending counts as a drop, and the snapshot goes out on the first sample after (`anchor-delta-frames` = 1); clock samples take a fixed 10-frame stride. The `window-closed` row now reports `snapshots/clocks/capture-lines/snapshot-dropped/clock-dropped/line-dropped`, and an offline case pins the pre-registered budgets (8/120/1,500) so a later edit cannot drift. Offline: 4 new capture cases plus the existing 40 pass under `/W4 /WX`; Release exits 0. Identity `CharacterPanel-scenegraph-v89-g5e4-source-capture-aaf7b009fd-aaf7b009fd-cl94faaed0c6-20261110T153008Z`, DLL 1,829,888 bytes SHA-256 `9e35db4c…970a32ca`, PDB `0378125a…a8b97ffd`, CodeView age 437. The E4 run INI `8864075e…` (`SourceTimeline=on`, `SourceTimelineGraph=DefaultFemale`, `SourcePoseCapture=true`) is prepared in `dist/`. [Build](docs/build.md#v89-g5e4-source-capture-2026-11-10).
- Pre-register [C-G5-E4](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e4), source-side pose and clock capture (2026-11-10, **documentation only — no code, no build, no run**): the source side still has no pose or clip-clock data at all, which blocks both the pose oracle and E2-C's source half, so one capture serves both. **No new detour**: the rows are produced by `SourceTimeline`'s existing post-UI sample, where `poseLocal` holds the frame's completed output, and the types are read through CommonLib (`hkbCharacter::poseLocal`/`numPoseLocal`, `hkQsTransform`), never guessed offsets — this keeps the E1 "instruments do not stack" rule intact instead of adding a 63856 hook beside the timeline. Two row kinds: a `pose-snapshot` block at settle anchors (pointer, `n`, every track's trans/rot/scale plus the active clip table with name/localTime/mode/speed/eff-duration) and a `clock-sample` row every 10 frames for the rate. The anchor rule is fixed before any value is seen: exactly one snapshot per settle, taken at the first post-UI frame at or after the settle's `tsc`, with `anchor-delta-frames` recorded and bounded to {0,1}; a second snapshot for the same input voids the window. Budgets are declared up front (8 blocks, 120 clock rows, 1,500 lines, roughly 1,120 used at 20 s) and any drop is `insufficient evidence`, never a silent truncation. Criteria S1–S6 cover anchor completeness, same-generation identity, per-track coverage, phase recordability, zero loss and computable rates; **they certify a qualified snapshot, not pose equality** — tolerance and calibration stay in gate 3. Known limits recorded: clip identity is still keyed by name, source-side weights/layers stay uncovered, the snapshot is ≤1 frame after the apply, and scale is read as stored. `SourcePoseCapture` requires `SourceTimeline=on` and is mutually exclusive with `SamplingLiveness`. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e4).
- Pre-register [C-G5-E2-C](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2-c), the playback-speed and clock ledger (2026-11-10, **documentation only — no code, no build, no run**): G5 asks for lifecycle, loop unwrapping, start/stop, single-play end, playback mode/speed, enforced duration, crop and the actual dt per stable clip instance, explicitly not requiring every active clip to advance exactly 1/60 per step. The contract fixes the sides first, because the source half has no clock capture at all (`SourceTimeline` reads words and node counts only) and is therefore **blocked on the same source-side 63856 instrument E4 needs** — only the candidate ledger is delivered. It then pins the decode of `clip-steps` against the source (`0`–`9` = 1–9 frames, `A`–`Z` = 10–35, `?` = more than 35, `N` = negative, `-` = absent) and the rules R1–R6, including batch delivery (totals count, single steps need not be 1/60) and the rule that single-play clips stop at `duration`. Criteria T1–T5 cover coverage, the single-play hold, loop wrap explainability, the advance-window rate and encoding legality. **First candidate ledger, from the C run of the same day**: `H2H_Equip` advances 49 frames over its 49-step advance window (**1.000×**) after delivering 12 then 15/15/7, holds 12 steps at `duration` (the `clip-clock` step-60 row reads 0.8333 = `duration`), while `H2H_Idle`, `1HM_Idle` and `SCAR_1hmReadyDummy` each advance exactly 72 frames in 72 steps (**1.000×**) with the historical 15-frame batching, and `MT_Idle` is present only at step 0. The rate denominator is the advance window, not every active step — a decode of the raw bit string caught that `H2H_Equip` is active for 61 steps but advances in only 49, and the contract says so. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2-c).
- Close the E3 pairing ledger (2026-11-10, **v88; R attempt 2 plus C run 1, `paired=2 missing=0`**): the candidate run armed the observe-only input probe and the unequip slice, drove `step=0..120` in both windows with no gaps or duplicates, and every endpoint matches the source: equip `step=0 default=10 nodes=113` (source `currentDefaultState 0->10`, `iRightHandEquipped 0->1` with `iState 0->9`) and `default=1` from step 49 (source settle `10->1`); unequip `step=0 default=12 nodes=126` (source `1->12` with `IsUnequipping` and `bEquipUnequip`) and, after the last `12` at step 20, `default=0` from step 21 (source `12->0`). P1–P5 all hold: both runs clean, each signature unique, both `step=0` endpoints on the same values, `paired + missing = 2` with log line numbers, and no cross-run clock anywhere in the ledger. C2 is recorded for the candidate only (`clip-ref` at steps 0/60/120 with names, binding indices, durations, modes and speeds). **Boundary**: the ledger proves comparability, not pose equality — the source still has no `poseLocal` capture and no clip identity, so the pose oracle needs its own contract; the candidate leaving the unequip state at step 21 against the source's 1.26 s is filed for the clock ledger (E2-C). The INI is back to dormant `be26ac09…` (10,667 bytes, all experiment keys off, the new selector key present). [Ledger](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-v2), [C run](docs/build.md#v88-g5e3-c1-2026-11-10).

- Name both source graphs and fix the first-person filter as v88 (2026-11-10, **built and deployed; R rerun next**): the v87 census read `found=2` with `DefaultFemale` 652/106 and `FirstPerson` 463/36, which identifies the pairing run's 463/36 as the **first-person** graph and the candidate source as `DefaultFemale`. Root cause: every historical resolver filtered with the case-sensitive lowercase `strstr(name, "1stperson")`, and the real project name is `FirstPerson`, so the filter never fired and the legacy "last non-1stperson graph" rule selected the first-person graph; `concurrent` windows were unaffected because they follow the experiment-pinned pointer. `source_graph_is_first_person()` now matches `firstperson` and `1stperson` case-insensitively and is used by the timeline collector, the event capture and the write capture; the product path is untouched. Recorded as a **scope correction, not a re-judgement**: G3-A's v37–v41 trajectory, G3B-E2's write observation (`writes=0`, already instrument-invalid) and E3-R attempt 1 all observed `FirstPerson`, so their "source graph" wording becomes "first-person graph" while their phase-sequence conclusions stand; G4-C1's concurrent zero-events conclusion is not affected. Offline: a sixth e3 case covers both spellings and the non-matches; all nine suites pass under `/W4 /WX`; Release exits 0. - Implement and deploy the E3 graph selector as v87 (2026-11-10, **built and deployed; census run next**): one actor owns more than one managed non-1stperson graph and the `on` resolver kept the last match, so the 2026-11-10 pairing run watched 463/36 while the candidate source is 652/106. `SourceTimeline=on` now prints one `graph-census` line per window (`found/shown/selector/origin/chosen`) plus one line per managed graph with its project name, word count and active-node count, and honours `SourceTimelineGraph=<projectName>` — case-insensitive, exact length, empty keeps the legacy rule, and a miss falls back visibly (`origin=selector-miss-fallback`) instead of silently swapping the graph. `concurrent` still observes the pinned graph and prints no census, so v86's pinning is untouched. Offline: the e3 suite grew to 5 cases (match, case folding, prefix rejection, empty selector, no-pick paths) and all 39 engine-free cases pass under `/W4 /WX`; Release exits 0. [Build](docs/build.md#v87-g5e3-graph-select-2026-11-10), [contract §八](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-v2).
- Run E3-R attempt 1 and stop it on the identity check (2026-11-10 22:51, v86, log 24,192 bytes `4cedd700…`, zero `[E]`): F7 opened the source window and it expired with `lines=174`, well inside the budget, and **all three required signatures appeared exactly once** — `currentDefaultState 0->10`, `iRightHandEquipped 0->1` with `iState 0->9`, and `currentDefaultState 1->12` — so the script and the settle rule both work. What disqualifies the round is the graph: `window-open … words=463 nodes=36` against the candidate source's `652/106`. Two graphs belong to the same actor and `player_graph()` returns the last non-1stperson one while the experiment is handed a different object in the same manager — the split v66 already recorded. The contract's stop condition (differing `words/nodes` across runs) therefore ends the round before any ledger, and P1–P5 are not judged. Proposed fix, not implemented: an `on`-only graph census line at window-open (index / `projectName` / words / nodes per managed non-1stperson graph) plus a `SourceTimelineGraph=<projectName>` selector whose empty value keeps today's behaviour — read-only, no detour, no product-path change. [Attempt record](docs/build.md#v86-g5e3-r1-2026-11-10), [contract §八](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-v2).
- Pre-register [C-G5-E3 v2](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-v2), ordinal input pairing (2026-11-10, **documentation only — no code, no build, no deployment, no run**): with the concurrent axis measured empty, pairing returns to the contract's original main axis, the fixed input history. Two runs replace one window — R runs natural play with `SourceTimeline=on` (F7, 20 s) through a fixed script (wait, equip a one-handed weapon, wait, unequip, wait), C runs the panel with `IndependentGraph=inject`, `CandidateInputProbe=true` and an **empty** `CandidateInputVariable` so the sampler arms observe-only and writes nothing, plus `CandidateUnequipProbe=true`/`CandidateUnequipEvent=Unequip`, with both display keys off. The pair key is `(k, input, event id)`: the source side must show a unique signature per input (`currentDefaultState 0→10`, `iRightHandEquipped|iState 0→1|0→9`, `currentDefaultState 1→12`) and the candidate must show a `step=0` row whose `default` matches that endpoint; `S_k` is fixed at 120/121 before any error is looked at. No cross-run clock is allowed — the source `tsc=` and the candidate `step` are separate domains, and any wall-clock/TSC alignment disqualifies the pair. Missing signatures, a missing `step=0`, budget exhaustion or any `[E]` stop the round instead of degrading it. P1–P5 cover run qualification, signature uniqueness, endpoint coverage, a complete `paired + missing` ledger with log line numbers, and the no-time-alignment rule. Explicitly not covered and required in any conclusion: **source-side clip identity** (so C2 cannot be called closed), the pose oracle (it needs a separate source-side `poseLocal` capture through the probe's `on` lifecycle), R1 diagnostics, and the concurrent window as an anchor. **Impact is zero code** — every row already ships: R's settle lines carry `tsc=`, and C's observe-only probe prints per-step `CANDIDATE INPUT … step= istate= default= nodes=`, `clip-ref` at 0/60/120 and the `CANDIDATE UNEQUIP … step=` rows. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-v2).
- Adjudicate E3 run 1 (2026-11-10 22:30, v86, log 358,712 bytes `eba02d06…`, zero `[E]`) — **stop at the first fork**: the timeline armed concurrently on the pinned graph (`words=652 nodes=106`, the same identity v66 pinned), and closed at `display-close` with **`lines=0`** across 316 display frames, i.e. not one settled variable and not one node-count change. The manifest is otherwise healthy (`steps=377`, `steps-saturated=no`, `tsc-per-second=4.29e9`, ordered first/last stamps) but reads `source-settles=0 paired=0 unpaired=0`, so the pre-registered order stops before any C1–C4 ledger and no G5 conclusion is drawn. This turns E1's indirect evidence into a direct measurement: while the panel is open the pinned source graph does not advance at all. Consequence: C3 must pair on the recorded input history — a source run under `SourceTimeline=on` with a fixed input script, a candidate run injecting the same script, and ordinal pairing (k-th input's settled snapshot against the k-th post-injection step range) with no wall-clock or TSC comparison across runs; v86's `step=`, `manifest steps/tsc` and settle `tsc=` stay useful as independent evidence, just not as a concurrent anchor. Secondary readings, recorded not adjudicated: R-1a cut producer drops from v85's 164 to **13** (contention, `full-dropped=0`, still not zero so `transport-complete=no`), E1 v3 reproduced a seventh time (`apply-paths` Σ 79,491 = `apply-entries`, `un=0`, 377/377 pairs, `p63563=9,485`), and the candidate stayed on the fallback branch (`e2a-main-frames=0`). INI restored to dormant `26625f09…` (10,092 bytes, all experiment keys off); the run INI is kept at `dist/CharacterPanel-e3-run-20261109.ini`. [Run record](docs/build.md#v86-g5e3-run1-2026-11-10), [reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-r).

The run INI `68be6ee9…` (10,105 bytes, no BOM, all CRLF) changes exactly one value - `SourceTimeline=false → concurrent` - plus five comment lines documenting the tri-state; 13 run keys were read back at the destination (`IndependentGraph=inject`, `DisplayPose/DisplayStep=true`, `SamplingLiveness=concurrent`, `SourceTimeline=concurrent`, everything else off). Nothing on the product path changed: both instrument keys are read-only. **One two-segment session is next**, and its first fork is `source-settles`: non-zero means the source graph still advances while the panel is open and the concurrent pairing axis holds; zero means that axis is structurally empty and C3 must pair on the recorded input history instead. [Deploy record](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#deploy-state), [build](docs/build.md#v86-g5e3-manifest-2026-11-09).
- Implement the approved E3 review as v86 (2026-11-09, **built but not deployed**): M1 gives `SourceTimeline` a third `concurrent` state - parsed tri-state with unknown values falling back to off, armed by the experiment on the pinned source graph before its dispatches and closed at the display boundary, with the sampler re-validating the pointer against the manager list (comparison only) and aborting on replacement. The probe install guard still refuses the legacy `on` lifecycle, so the instruments cannot stack. M2 puts both sides on `__rdtsc()`: the probe keeps a 2048-step timeline (saturation reported, never wrapped), `e2a` rows gain `step=`, source settle rows gain `tsc=` and publish into a 64-slot ring, and the close sequence emits one `manifest` row that drains that ring and counts `paired`/`unpaired` with the pre-registered rule (first candidate step at or after the settle confirmation, one step per settle, no interpolation, no nearest-neighbour choice); `tsc-per-second` is printed for readability only and never feeds the decision. M3 adds no foot-IK anchor, per the review. R-1a turns the producer gate into a bounded spin of up to 64 pauses with the drop counted once and `full-dropped` still separate; R-1b's per-tid second-chance slot is deliberately deferred until this reading shows it is needed. `ProbeRecord` stays at 64 bytes, with no new detour, INI key or per-frame record type; the two instruments must both be `concurrent` or only one installs. Offline: 4 new engine-free cases (tri-state parse with typo rejection, the pairing rule with no-reuse and late-settle cases, gate contention counted once, settle-ring order/overflow/reset) plus the existing 34 pass under `/W4 /WX`, and the Release build exits 0. Identity `CharacterPanel-scenegraph-v86-g5e3-manifest-1e645a6d48-1e645a6d48-cl94faaed0c6-20261109T145833Z`, DLL 1,812,992 bytes SHA-256 `57a2b11a…1eea645`, PDB `04f12c17…375d6805`, CodeView age 431. **Deployment and the run are a separate step.** [Review](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-r), [build](docs/build.md#v86-g5e3-manifest-2026-11-09).
- Pre-review the three E3 implementation questions (2026-11-09, **documentation only — no code, no build, no deployment, no run**): [C-G5-E3-R](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-r) answers what the E3 contract left open and overturns two readings from the E2-B record. (1) `ring-dropped` is producer contention, not ring capacity: both sessions report `full-dropped=0` while `ring-dropped == contention-dropped` (121 for v84, 164 for v85), and the loss comes from the try-only admission in `ProbePushGate::try_enter`, so enlarging the 4,096-entry ring fixes nothing; a window that always drops ~1.9% would make C4's "collection loss ⇒ insufficient evidence" fire every run, so the review proposes a bounded spin plus a per-tid second-chance slot and rejects pre-registering an acceptable drop rate as a relaxation. (2) The `b-*` foot-IK counters are process-wide, not candidate-scoped: `record_footik_entry` filters neither by graph nor by out block, and the 217 visible v85 `b-entry` rows span six threads and 18 distinct `out` addresses (tracks 116×188, 98×29), so "foot IK dominates poseLocal" is downgraded to a magnitude reference and B5's attribution closure has no evidence base — which is why the review advises **against** making a foot-IK sequence a manifest anchor. Three proposals stand for approval: M1 gives `SourceTimeline` a third `concurrent` state instead of lifting the deliberate instrument mutex (the timeline installs no detour, so the exclusion is policy, and v64's `SourceEventCapture` already shows the shape), armed from the experiment with the pinned source graph and closed at display-close, replacing the `player_graph` resolver that failed v64 attempt 1; M2 pairs on a TSC-stamped input history — the first candidate step at or after source settle confirmation, no interpolation, no nearest-neighbour choice, missing stays missing — carrying `epoch/request/view` on one manifest row per request and `step` on the existing `e2a` row rather than adding per-frame record types; M3 drops the foot-IK anchor outright. The minimal surface adds no detour, no INI key and no per-frame record type, keeps `ProbeRecord` at 64 bytes (anti-pattern 16), and lists five offline cases including the v66 unqualified pair as a hard stop. **Implementation stays unauthorised** until M1–M3 and the R-1 disposal are approved. [Review](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3-r).
- Record the session summary and current state (2026-11-08, documentation): E2 fully closed (E2-A′ identity convergence attributing every residual to the three enumerable post-process chains; E2-B observing foot IK as the dominant poseLocal writer at ~2.7 calls/frame and ~184 components rewritten per call, plus the new 116/98 out-track dual values), two crash fixes (v81's volatile byte-wise ring copy; v84's out-pointer dereference), two new anti-patterns (alignas is a promise not a repair — verify by re-disassembly; hook argument layouts must be derived from the consumer side), E3 pre-registered with its four deliverables and the manifest-index gap, and gate-1 progress E1 ✅ / E2 ✅ / E3 contract ready / joint acceptance pending. Deployed v85 `e5552e89…`, dormant INI `27f481f5…`, branch synced through `2dea193`. The three open design questions for the E3 implementation review: manifest index scheme vs the instrument mutual-exclusion discipline, the time-axis alignment between source settled snapshots and candidate step sequences, and the dual foot-IK call-sequence anchors. [Summary](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#status-20261108-session).
- Pre-register [C-G5-E3](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3), the natural-source pairing contract (2026-11-08, **documentation only**): E3 must produce a qualified same-generation source/candidate pair so the pose comparison has a credible reference. E2-B's finding carries over as a constraint — the out-block track count takes two values (116/98), so the manifest must record both sides' foot-IK call counts and scopes, and source/candidate are inherently cross-thread so pairing follows the manifest's input/time axis rather than threads. Four deliverables: C1 source qualification (fixed save/equipment/skeleton/OAR config, natural progression, control runs separating natural evolution from candidate interference), C2 bound-resource identity including OAR replacement/variants (never just animationName or the shared binding set), C3 the pairing manifest fixed **before** any pose-error look with quantisation and ambiguity rules (v66's unqualified `12220/SVFI/13.06` vs `1022/original/1.8667` pair registered as the counter-example), and C4 full same-generation records plus a missing-data ledger where candidate defects are measured differences rather than dropped frames. R1 stays diagnostic-only; no candidate hash repetition substitutes for a source oracle. Offline pre-check: both sides' recorders exist (SourceTimeline, the E2 probes); the gap is a shared manifest index (epoch/request/view plus both foot-IK call sequences). No implementation authorised; gate 1 stays open until the joint acceptance. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3).
- Complete E2-B with the v85 session (2026-11-08 20:00, log 369,541 bytes `6d20f0f2…`): the restored `b-entry`/`b-return` rows appeared (2,657/2,659 at close, 217/213 visible), `b-calls=2,736`, `b-compared=2,724`, `b-overwritten=0` (B1), `b-rewritten=501,806` (~184 per call, `b-max-delta=10.23`) with a two-tier per-frame distribution of ~10–33 minor corrections versus 336–533 full rewrites (B2), and `tracks-entry == tracks-return` on all 213 visible rows (B6). The faulting load reads the **source** on the stack while the ring-slot stores are `movups`, so declaring alignment only made the compiler more confident about `movaps`. v81 `f7382f59cd` (`2fcb029c…23b7b7`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `68d57d1e…`, CodeView age 420) copies records volatile byte-wise via `copy_probe_record` and drops the two 16-byte static asserts. **Offline proof**: scanning `.text` for the ring pattern now finds zero `movaps`/`movups` near it (previously 2 + 6). Corrected anti-pattern: `alignas` is a promise to the compiler, not a repair — for frames entered through a hand-written thunk, either make the copy unvectorisable or fix the thunk and **verify by re-disassembly**, never by a passing static_assert. At 64 bytes per record the compiler vectorises the whole-record store with `movaps`, which needs 16-byte alignment, but `ProbeRecord`'s widest member is `uintptr_t` so it only had 8 and the static `ProbeState` carried that into the array. v79's two extra `uint16` counters made the record exactly 64 bytes — flipping `movups` to `movaps` and exposing the latent defect (v78's 60-byte record never triggered it). New anti-pattern: record layout changes flip the compiler's copy instructions, so alignment must be explicit and asserted for PODs that are bulk-copied inside static or heap containers. Offline verification before the contract pinned the exact chain: 63582 takes `n = min(slot1.numData, slot0.numData, numAnim)` and calls `64112(count=n, A=slot1 tracks, B=slot0 tracks, out tracks, w)`, where `64112` mixes only `trans` and `rot` per 0x30-byte track — `trans = (1−w)·A + w·B`, `rot = normalize((1−w)·A + w·B′)` with `B′ = −B` when `dot<0` and an `rsqrtss`-plus-Newton normalisation (scale is not mixed; left for E2-B) — and the weight passed in is `[g+0x230]`, not 63581's internal `alpha`. The `[g+0x148]` overwrite lands in the payload while the memcpy source is the track array, so it cannot disturb poseLocal on the main path, and the fallback branch sets `[g+0x230] = 1.0f`, which makes the same identity degenerate to `slot0` without an exception. - Implement the C-G5-E1 liveness probe and fix the detour thunk defect it exposed (2026-11-03, **source + engine-free cases; no DLL, no identity, no deployment, no game run**): the probe adds [probe_liveness.cpp](src/animation/behavior_graph/probe_liveness.cpp) plus [probe_liveness_policy.h](src/animation/behavior_graph/probe_liveness_policy.h) (`PLUGIN_SOURCES` 32 → 33), two read-only detours on 63589 (sampling entry, head `40 56 57 41 56`) and 63856 (poseLocal last writer, head `48 81 EC 88 00 00 00`), a per-frame read-only queue snapshot (`*(void**)0x31be0d0`, byte `+0x430`), the `[Experiment] SamplingLiveness=off|on|concurrent` key and the lifecycle wiring (install at `kDataLoaded`, invalidate on load/new game, per-frame drain in `render.cpp`, arm in the experiment, close at the display boundary). While writing its layout case, the production thunk bytes were executed and the previous thunk generation was found to read its saved entry arguments one slot too low: `[rsp+0x38/0x30/0x28]` were used *before* the `sub rsp,0x20` they assumed, so `record_source_event` received the caller's `rax` as its behavior pointer and `record_setter` received it as its graph pointer. [Audit30](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit30) records the proof (`existing-slot-layout: recorder got [0x1866a180000, 0x1111, 0x2222]`), the blast radius (58377 event capture and 63607/63609 setter capture affected; the OAR condition detours use Microsoft Detours and the direct 58377 dispatches are unaffected) and the rulings: **C-G3B-E2's `writes=0` and C-G4-C1's `events=0`/`other-graph=55` are instrument-invalid**, `calls=62` survives because it is counted before the gate, and **G4-C is reopened** until a fixed instrument is re-run. All three encoders now push eight registers, reserve the shadow space first and read `+0x50/+0x48/+0x40` (`+0x68` for the return address), with `rsp ≡ 0 (mod 16)` at the call; `source_write_capture` also allocates two strides instead of writing 19 bytes past its block. Seven engine-free cases pass under `cl /std:c++latest /W4 /WX` (`7 probe-liveness cases passed`), including one that executes the production thunk bytes and asserts the entry arguments and return address. The Release build first failed at link; both causes were environment-side and are recorded with the repair in the [v67 build record](docs/build.md#v67-g5e1-liveness-2026-11-03): `DETOURS_LIBRARY` resolved to the package's **debug** lib after a clean configure (fixed by passing the release path), and the vcpkg `spdlog.lib` references three STL internals that the installed CRT import libraries do not export (fixed by linking `spdlog::spdlog_header_only`, with a note in CMakeLists; rebuilding spdlog and disabling vectorized algorithms did not help). Configure also needed `-DFETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED=ON` because CommonLibSSE re-populates `hde64` from GitHub. The build now exits 0 with only the pre-existing D9025 and CommonLibSSE `hde64.c` warnings. Clean-tree identity `CharacterPanel-scenegraph-v67-g5e1-liveness-7dd5ea7458-cl94faaed0c6-20261103T155041Z`: DLL 1,501,696 bytes SHA-256 `e725066c…3457e`, PDB 48,902,144 bytes SHA-256 `1a4da885…7bc4`, CodeView `{7F586C2E-…}` age 395, all three `SKSEPlugin_*` exports present. **Deployed 2026-11-04**: the v66 trio was backed up to `dist/deploy-backup-20261104-v66/`, the v67 DLL/PDB now sit in `E:\SkyrimAE\mods\CharacterPanel\SKSE\Plugins\`, and the run INI (`ff3c6f34…`, 9,465 bytes) sets `IndependentGraph=inject`, `DisplayPose=true`, `DisplayStep=true`, `SamplingLiveness=concurrent` with every other instrument off (keys asserted by read-back). After the run the dormant INI was restored from the backup.

- Pre-register the E1 minimal liveness probe for G5 gate 1 (2026-11-03, **documentation only — not implemented, built, deployed or run**): [C-G5-E1](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e1-probe) turns audit29's three open questions (which thread runs the frame path, whether the pinned source graph is actually sampled, whether a queue consumer re-enters sampling elsewhere) into pre-registered observations and criteria. Observation points are two read-only detours whose head bytes were verified against the fixed image — 63589 `0xbb2a40` (`40 56 57 41 56`, a 5-byte jmp with no padding, boundary at +5) and 63856 `0xbc2fe0` (`48 81 EC 88 00 00 00`, 7-byte patch with two pads, `sub rsp,0x88` relocated) — plus a per-frame read-only snapshot of `*(void**)0x31be0d0` and `byte[q+0x430]`. Attribution is exact, not inferred: 63856's second argument is `&g+0x160`, so `graph = rdx − 0x160` (call sites `0xbb2af7` in 63589 and `0xbaebd8` in 63568), and the caller return address separates the engine's local loop (`0xba3f6e` inside 63358) from the plugin's own `GenerationEntryID`/`SamplingBranchID` calls. Pre-registered criteria P1–P5 fix install/liveness, source hit with a plugin-call positive control, pairing integrity (presence and order, not a 100% ratio, because `63564`-true and `numAnim==0` are legal early returns), thread uniqueness (two tids for the pinned graph is reported as a refutation), and the queue flag with frames; a *negative* result requires a complete window with a working positive control and no drops, otherwise the window is invalid. Hard constraints: do not add hooks at 32898/63358, never stack with the other detour probes (`SourceTimeline`/`SourceEventCapture` in particular), no new native calls or writes, one window per process. Implementation impact is bounded to `probe_liveness.{h,cpp}` plus `probe_liveness_policy.h` (`PLUGIN_SOURCES` 32 → 33), one three-state INI key `SamplingLiveness`, and engine-free regression cases; audit29's poseLocal offsets were corrected to graph-relative `g+0x150/+0x158/+0x15d` (character-relative `+0x90/+0x98/+0x9d` from `character = g+0xc0`) while designing the probe. Gate 1 stays NOT_READY.

- Complete the E1 static source-scheduling / output-lifetime audit for G5 gate 1 (2026-11-03, offline, **no code, no build, no run**): [audit29](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit29) pins down the frame path and its completion boundary from the fixed AE 1.6.1170 image. The per-graph sample has exactly one statically reachable entry — 32898 → 63358's local loop → 63589 — while 63358's dispatch branch is a `BSTMessageQueue<BSTSmartPointer<BSAnimationGraphManager>>` (`VTABLE___DeferredUpdate` at `0x1985ce0`, `CreateSemaphoreA(NULL,0,0x80,NULL)` into `+0x428`) gated on `q && byte[q+0x430]`, with a `Sleep(0)` retry and then `ReleaseSemaphore`. Its per-graph payload 63359 is **unreferenced in the whole image** (raw rel32 scan 0 hits; no 4-byte RVA, 8-byte VA or imm64; no vtable slot in the 7,451 mapped tables), so no thread model may be asserted from it. Generation is **not** in 63581: 63568 calls `58343` (generate), then `63573`, then 63581, then `63856`; 63581 does the dual-slot gate (`+0x1e8`/`+0x1ec`, max≥0 and min<0), `alpha = max/(max−min)`, `[g+0x234] ← [g+0x230]`, then `63666` → `63582` (n = min(param, numData slot0, slot1); `59303` plus per-track `64112` lerp with sign-corrected nlerp) → `[g+0x240] = n` → memcpy `n*0x30` into poseLocal → optional `58423`/`58427`. `63856` is the last poseLocal writer: it walks the node table at `g+0x160`, clamps the count by `[table+0x10]`, and writes rotation/translation only. Three counts must stay separate (capacity `[skeleton+0x30]` vs reference fill `[skeleton+0x40]` vs actual `min(...)`), and there is **no native output-completion sequence** — `+0x240` and `+0x230/+0x234` are overwritten every sample, so a completion counter can only be synthesized at a proven capture point after `63856`, on the same thread, inside one lifetime epoch. Forward correction: 58423's chained-unwind group is 7,731 bytes (`0xab7f30..0xab9d63`), so audit14's "0x65 bytes, another function follows" is wrong and its original "~0x1E00" reading was right. Toolchain re-established offline (`build/g5-e1-audit-20261103/`, ignored) and validated against audit28's 21 process groups with 0/21 mismatches. Gate 1 stays NOT_READY; next is the E1 minimal-probe implementation review.

Round 2 (`IsUnequipping`) is an INI-only redeploy of the same binary. Log archived ([72,238 bytes](docs/diagnostics/README.md#g3bs4-r1), SHA-256 `685ed235…`).

The contract pre-registers the ladder `bEquipUnequip` → `IsUnequipping` → `PitchManualOverride` — the only three variables that change at the source's unequip transition moment (53.652) — because the v41 timing rules out the equipped-state setters (they fire 0.85 s after the transition) as its trigger. The real equip/unequip flow therefore does not drive these phases through graph events: combined with v52 (`WeapEquip` injection does move the candidate's equip phase, so the event entry exists) and audit27's static external setter sites, the mechanism reading is that engine code writes graph variables directly and the state machine transitions on variable conditions. The remaining untested lead is the one pre-registered in the E1 contract: the **unequip-side variables** (`bEquipUnequip` index 125, `PitchManualOverride` index 65 — both proven present in the candidate's table) were never written as inputs; the next slice reuses the S3 two-phase structure (WeapEquip → settle gate) with a typed by-name variable write instead of the event dispatch. - Add the v21 pose-content decode for the v20a experiment (2026-10-27), the bounded follow-up to "the raw pose hash changed 120/120 times": at steps 0/60/120 the drive now reads every `character.poseLocal` entry through the declared `hkQsTransform` (0x30 stride, capped at 256), checks finiteness over translation xyz / rotation xyzw / scale xyz (the fourth lane is padding and excluded), compares each entry component-wise with the previous checkpoint at a 1e-6 threshold (a NaN operand yields an infinite delta so it can never read as unchanged), does the same for the source's own `poseLocal`, and counts candidate/source bit-identical entries as an aliasing watch. Four index-spread bones (`0 / ⅓ / ⅔ / last`, i.e. 0/38/76/115 for the observed 116-bone skeleton) get named rows via a bounded, plausibility-checked name read. The pre-registered verdict is staged: any non-finite entry stops the round (audit23's three poseLocal copy paths become the next question); `source-equal>0` stops as an aliasing suspicion; all-finite with `moved≥16` at 60 and 120 plus at least two of four sampled bones moving is "pose content valid" — which is still not a G2 pass — and `moved<16` would demote v20a's motion to padding/noise. - Record the first v20 runtime attempt (2026-10-27 21:48) and its pre-entry crash. The deployed `CharacterPanel-scenegraph-behavior-graph-v20-generation-entry-20261027-a83e5229e7-cl94faaed0c6-20261027T134152Z` loaded on exact AE 1.6.1170 with `SCENEGRAPH BUILD` matching the package manifest, the single private-state guard passed (`GRAPH ENTRY … outcome=ready reason=ready entry=63589 mode=0`), and the thread then faulted with `EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION` at `CharacterPanel.dll+0x28067`, instruction `mov r8, [rbx+0x88]`, `independent_graph.cpp:955` — the v16-era read-only g3-gates diagnostic whose chain starts at `[ctx+0x50]` although `sizeof(RE::hkbContext)==0x50`, i.e. one past the context buffer. G2 has not passed.

- Add the twenty-first 1.6.1170 binary audit (2026-10-27), which settles why the recipe never generated: 63602's whole generation block is guarded by the first argument's `behaviorGraph` (`0xbb62ae: cmp qword [r13+0x208],0; je 0xbb63bd`), and the block contains both the 65149 generation call and 63602's internal 63587 call — so on the unloaded scratch graph neither call happens at all (stronger than audit 18's "returns immediately"). `65177` (0xc263c0) is a name joiner: it builds `<[object+0x38]>/<[buffer+0x38]>` from tagged string pointers (low bit masked) into a stack hkStringPtr-like builder (`+0x108` data, `+0x110` size, `+0x112` capacity); `59268` (0xacf770) is the two-instruction leaf `movss [rcx+0xF0], xmm1; ret`. 63602's "buffer" is a **descriptor of twelve stack sub-object pointers** starting at `[rbp+0x140]` (each 8-byte slot filled with `&[rbp+...]`), so audit 20's reading of `+0x00/+0x08` as embedded fields holds only for the other sink class used by 0xba4e30 (whose first field is a BSFixedString assigned by 69166). Conclusion: there is no safe minimal generation experiment left — getting engine-generated pose for the candidate requires either putting the candidate's real behaviorGraph into 63602's first argument (unverified side effects on a real graph) or registering the candidate in a manager (breaking the G1 no-registration boundary). That is an architecture decision, not another observation; audit only, no game run, no recipe change. See [audit](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit21) and [next questions](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#next-audit).

- Add the twentieth 1.6.1170 binary audit (2026-10-27), which decodes the sink adapter's slot-5 (`0xc25ee0`, the 65149 adapter) and the buffer it consumes. Slot-5 first calls `65177([ctx+8], buffer+0x38, &local_0x118)` to resolve a name/state, then builds a `BSFixedString` key (static empty when the local name is null) and looks it up in the hash at `[[adapter+8]+8]+0x28` (capacity `+0xC`, sentinel `+0x18`), reads the entry's float at `+0x8` and calls `59268(buffer, float)`; it then reads `buffer+0x88`. The buffer's known fields: `+0x00` a refcounted project-name string (assigned by `69166`, which is a refcounted string assign whose release goes through `Entry::release8` at 0xced9a0), `+0x08` a pointer to the name→float registry, `+0x38` the name/state sub-object read by 65177, `+0x88` another pointer; the total is at least 0xA0 bytes (63602's stack buffer at `[rbp+0x140]`). Consequence: fabricating a sink buffer is not acceptable — it would corrupt a refcounted string and violate unknown lifetimes — so the v20 "explicit generation" contract is parked. The next bounded choice is either audit21 (decode 65177/59268 and the registry source) or a read-only observation contract that samples a real engine sink without constructing or writing one. Audit only: no game run, no recipe change. See [audit](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit20) and [next questions](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#next-audit).

- Add the nineteenth 1.6.1170 binary audit (2026-10-27), which pins the engine's real per-frame generation context. The chain is `0x53fd20` (outer, sole caller of the next) → `0xba4e30` (picks `graph = [mgr+0x48][index]` with `[mgr+0x50]` count and `[mgr+0xb0]` default index, calls 69166 with `graph+0x1F0` = projectName, then 63595) → `63595` (0xbb31b0; gate `[graph+0x200] != 0` = `ProjectDBData*`, then resolves the request's id array against the `projectDBData+0xc8` hash — capacity `+0xAC`, sentinel `+0xB8` — and only calls 63859 when the resolved count matches) → `63859` (0xbc32d0) → 65232 → 65237. The sink is a 0x10-byte adapter `{vtable, buffer}`: three known vtables (0x1992da8 built inside 65149/63602, 0x1989320 for 0xbc3270, 0x1989360 for 0xbc32d0), slots 1-4 are four `mov al,1; ret` predicates, slot0 is the deleting destructor and **slot5 is the output-forwarding virtual** (`0xc25ee0`/`0xbc51c0`/`0xbc72e0`). 63602's own sink buffer is its stack local at `[rbp+0x140]` (fields up to `[rbp+0x1e0]`/`[rbp+0x1f4]`, roughly 0xA0 bytes). Consequence: generation cannot be obtained by calling 63602 or 63587; it needs `(graph, request, sink)` or `(character, values, sink)` with a valid adapter/buffer, and the candidate's `ProjectDBData` gate already passes while `behaviorGraph.isActive`/`numStaticNodes` remain uncollected. The next bounded question is audit20 (the slot-5 buffer layout and the `0x53fd20` driver) before any explicit-generation contract. Audit only: no game run, no recipe change. See [audit](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit19) and [next questions](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#next-audit).

- Add the eighteenth 1.6.1170 binary audit (2026-10-26/27), which corrects the long-standing attribution of the per-step pipeline: 63602 (0xbb4c50) binds `r13 = rcx` and performs all of its per-graph work — 65149 generation (`rcx = r13+0xC0`), 63587 update (`rcx = r13`), 63584 output/timer reset (`rcx = r13`) — on its **first** argument, while the second only receives vtable table writes (slots 0x30/0x38/0x40/0x48/0x50/0x60 with 0x28-stride table entries). The engine's own per-graph helper 0xbacc90 constructs a scratch graph (63555), calls 63602(scratch, target), then destroys it (63556) — exactly the recipe the plugin mirrors — so the first argument is an unloaded scratch graph whose `character.behaviorGraph` (`+0xC0+0x58`), `+0x110` and `+0x208` are null: 65149 returns at its first gate and 63587 at its prologue, so the pass generates nothing. The real generation entries are the character-level wrappers 0xbc3270/0xbc32d0, both gated on `character.behaviorGraph != 0` and calling 65232 (gate: `behaviorGraph.isActive (+0x12C)`; partition sizing from `numStaticNodes (+0x128)`) and then 65237; 0xbc3270 is vtable-only and 0xbc32d0 has a single caller in 0xbb31b0. 63584 (0xbb0710) resets `+0x1E8/+0x1EC` to `-MAXFLT` and `+0x230` to 0 on the same first argument. Consequence: v7–v19 never generated pose data for the candidate, so `generatorOutputs`'s `numData` staying 0 is the expected result, not a failing gate; the next bounded question is audit19 (the `0xbb31b0` caller and the sink/adapter layout at vtables 0x1989320/0x1989360). Audit only: no game run, no recipe change. See [audit](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit18) and [next questions](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#next-audit).

- Add the seventeenth 1.6.1170 binary audit (2026-10-26), which resolves the v19 no-arm: the arm block's step gate is `63864` (0xbc3990, sole call site 0xbb2603), a minimum over `hkbBehaviorGraph::activeNodes` (`+0x98`) of a normalised per-node animation state value (default FLT_MAX, per-node inputs `[node+0x64]/[node+0x68]/[node+0x72]/[node+0x106]` and the `[node+0x50]` array via `0xacf340`); on the candidate the returned value is ≤0.04583, so `xmm6 = ret - 0.5*dt` can never clear the `1.25*dt` arm threshold. The candidate's `rootNode` (+0x218) is zeroed by the `BShkbAnimationGraph` constructor (0xbad239, `r15=0`) and no audited function re-fills it, so `63575`'s distance path and the arm block's distance branch are both unavailable; who writes `rootNode` on a native graph remains UNVERIFIED (the per-function `+0x218` store sweep is documented but desyncs on the constructor block). Conclusion: the eye point was necessary but not sufficient — the missing input is animation selection/generation, not another record field — so the next bounded question is audit18 (the 65236/65237 generation entry and 63666's output selection). Audit only: no game run, no recipe change. See [audit](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit17) and [next questions](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#next-audit).

- Record the G2 v19 run and adjudication (2026-10-26 23:46 session): the loaded identity matched the build, all `GRAPH FRAME schema=v19` rows (30 + 1) carried `root218=0x0 b248=0` and a non-null `eye`, the run produced 40 INDEPENDENT GRAPH and 36 BEHAVIOR GRAPH rows with no `[E]` and no crash, `source-stable=true retained=true`, and the summary reported `arm-observed=no` with the pose unchanged at `bc160a8c2b3a5a58`. Adjudication: pre-registered branch 6 — the arm block's outer gates all passed (non-null eye, `+0x2A=0`, `+0x2B=1`, `[graph+0x248]=0`, step-0 pre-swap read slot `+4=21`/`+0x38=1.0`), so the stop is inside the step computation: with rootNode null the distance branch is skipped (`xmm2=FLT_MAX`) and the 0xbb2603 `63864()` normalisation replaces the 0.25 candidate (`xmm6 = 63864() - 0.5*dt` when the return is ≤0.25), leaving `xmm1 = xmm6 + t_larger ≤ 1.25*dt`; no timer was rewritten, so no Path B. New runtime facts: the candidate's `[graph+0x218] rootNode = 0` (the earlier non-null reading is withdrawn) and audit16's "null root means a fixed 0.25 step" is refuted. G2 remains NOT PASSED; the next bounded question is audit17 (`63864`'s return semantics and the missing candidate rootNode), with no eye-offset, dt, timer or weight changes authorised before it. - Add the G2 v19 pre-registered contract (design only, not implemented): a single-variable causal experiment that points the per-step `BSAnimationUpdateData::optionalEyePosition` at a read-only 3×float value (rootNode position +900 on Z, or a zero vector when rootNode is null) while leaving the call order, the 40-row `INDEPENDENT GRAPH` budget and the 30+1 `GRAPH FRAME` budget unchanged. It appends `root218/b248/eye` to existing `GRAPH FRAME` rows and `arm-observed` to the summary rather than adding row kinds. Six pre-registered branches are pinned (outer arm gate unreachable, armed-but-still-Path-A, Path B with the f gate still closed, full chain, crash/assertion, arming internal gate), and the contract states that the arming window is effectively only step 0 because the `+0x2C=1` old-read-slot `f>0` gate is satisfied solely by the step-0 slot-2 write — a failed step 0 stops the round without parameter tweaks. No code, build, package, deployment or run is included; G2 acceptance is unchanged. See [contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#g2-v19-contract).

- Add the sixteenth 1.6.1170 binary audit (2026-10-26), which explains the v18 five-stage observation at instruction level: the update record is `RE::BSAnimationUpdateData`, whose `+0x18` is `optionalEyePosition` (`NiPoint3*`) and whose only writer is the REFR-family vtable slot 0x3C8 `ModifyAnimationUpdateData` (`TESObjectREFR` 0x2f71a0 is a bare `ret 0` stub, `Actor` 0x6a3750 fills the eye/face node +0xa0 or null, `PlayerCharacter` 0x74e7a0 overrides). 63587 has exactly two call sites (manager loop 0xba3dd5, 63602 tail 0xbb63b8); the engine's own per-frame producer 0x54c130 zeroes `+0x10..+0x1F` and never calls slot 0x3C8, while the seven external-record sites let their REFR fill the record before 0x54c1f0. The Path B fork condition is corrected to `record+0x2A==0 && t_larger>=0 && t_smaller>-MAXFLT` (no upper bound; `{0,-MAXFLT}` fails even at dt=0, so the earlier "dt=0 is the only statically satisfiable form" reading is withdrawn). The t-arming block (0xbb252b) needs a non-null `+0x18`, `+0x2B!=0`, `[graph+0x248]==0` and the `+0x2C`/slot conditions; it writes `t_smaller = t_larger + step` and jumps past the reset, while the reset (0xbb2662) runs only while `-t_larger>=0` and clears both slots' onFraction/numData. 63581's gate requires both slots `+4>2` **and** `+0x38>0` plus `t_larger>=0`/`t_smaller<0`; `f=1.0` is written only by 63582 (63568 only back-fills `+0x32`), and 63575's count also depends on the eye position. Consequence: the candidate's `+0x18=null` pins t at the reset value, forces Path A on every step, and that reset clears the weights — v18's observations now have a machine-code explanation instead of an open hypothesis. The audit retires audit 15's "engine per-frame record (transform=null) → Path B" and "63581 gates pass statically after a Path A reset" claims. Audit only: no game run and no source/behaviour change; the bounded next step is a v19 causal-experiment contract that supplies an eye position (not implemented). See [audit](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md#audit16) and [next questions](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#next-audit).

Three changes to the [drive loop](src/animation/behavior_graph/independent_graph.cpp) behind the unchanged `IndependentGraph=inject` setting, observation budget held at 40 rows (only the `phase=slot2` row gains a `removed=` field): **(1) Path B record** — the per-step 63587 record switches from v15's 63602-tail copy (`+0x2A=1`, a slot-5 zero-dt pipeline special) to the manager's real per-frame producer 0x54c130 byte map (audit 15 Q4): `float dt=1/60` at +0x00, `+0x08..+0x27` zero, `u16 +0x28 id=0`, `+0x2A=0` (Path B — no double-buffer swap, no track reset, 0x246 cleared, `[0x240]` recomputed via 63575), `+0x2B=1`, `+0x2C=1`, `+0x2E=1`; **(2) predicate flip** — slot 3 (63622, RemoveRagdollFromWorld) moves to immediately after the slot-2 call, before the drive loop, undoing slot 2's per-body world registration while the physics-world borrow is still in place so `rigidBodies[0]->world` is null at every sampling moment and 63564 returns false (audit 15 Q1: the only non-chain-breaking flip on the candidate; v12 proved slot 3 leaves the track headers intact); the post-loop slot-3 call stays as an idempotent re-check feeding the existing `phase=pipeline` `ragdoll-removed=` field; **(3) direct 63581** — the per-step sampling entry 63589 is retired (its b246 fork was the very detour v16 pinned) and the sampling block is reached by a direct `63581(candidate, word[graph+0x240])` call (`SamplingBranchID`; audit 15 Q2's two-live-argument convention, second argument read from the plugin's existing per-step mirror field after the mirror write); `sampling_entry`, `SamplingEntryID` and `mode_byte` are removed with zero dangling references. Pre-registered criteria ①–⑤ in the contract section: ① `pred=0x00` + pose triple diverges (source stable, no crash) → G2 motion evidence achieved (PASSED on the motion criterion), next = OAR integration gates with a stability rerun; ② `pred=0x00` pose still frozen → audit 16 instrumenting 63581 internals (63666 output / 63582 mix / 58423 gates); ③ `pred=0x01` → audit the re-registration path; ④ `post-update1` without the Path B signature `w245=0x0000 b246=0` → re-review before any further step; ⑤ no crash, source stable; early-step gate ②/③ failures of 63581 (the `[0x228]` slot's onFraction may still be 0.0 before the pipeline fills it) are pre-registered as expected transients. Expected new signatures: `post-update1` shows `w245=0x0000 b246=0 num-anim=0x0074` (Path B recomputes it) and `phase=slots` pointers no longer alternate (no swap). Built as `CharacterPanel-scenegraph-scenegraph-a32766a080+dirty-cl94faaed0c6-20261025T160248Z` (DLL SHA-256 `904D6B5B…`, PDB `1B208E04…`), deployed on 2026-10-26 after backing the v16 pair up to `dist/deploy-backup-20261026-v16/`; the deployed INI was set to `inject` for the run adjudicated in the entry above and is back to `off` since.
- Record the G2 v16 run adjudication (2026-10-25) and add the binary-audit 15 section, "63564 谓词真值条件与 63581 采样入口约定（2026-10-25 续审计十五）" ([doc](docs/behavior-graph-construction-audit.md)). The run's raw log was archived verbatim as `CharacterPanel-g2v16-gates-20261025-2258.log` (byte-identical by `cmp`; 2026-10-25 22:58 session, build `…a32766a080+dirty-cl94faaed0c6-20261025T2245xx` per the v16 contract's recorded identity, DLL SHA-256 `A732AC08…`; request=1, actor 00000014, exactly 40 rows = the 40-row budget, no crash, `source-stable=true`). The decisive rows are verbatim — `phase=g3-gates g100=0x27073572de0 flag4a=0x00 ctx+50=null +40=null +88=null`, `phase=pred step=0 r=0x01`, `phase=pred step=60 r=0x01` — the reading: **pre-registered criterion ① landed, G1 confirmed** — the 63564 predicate returns true on the candidate at both checkpoints, so 63581 skips its entire sampling/mixing/pose-application block every step and the candidate pose stays bind-frozen (`bc160a8c2b3a5a58`×3); the predicate reads exactly the two fields the borrow window makes non-null (physicsWorld borrowed, ragdollDriver own), confirming the suspicion raised at contract time as the mechanism to defeat in v17. A compounding recipe fact (second gate in the same run): 63587's prologue writes `word[0x245]=0x101` every step and Path A never resets 0x246, so at sampling time `b246=1` every step → 63589 routes to 63568 (header back-fill only, dl=0 → return) — **63581 was never reached even absent the predicate gate** (supporting rows: `post-update1 … w245=0x101 b246=1 b249=1` matching v15; the step-120 slots row `p0=… n=0x0074 f=1.000 h0=48bfefcceccb91f1` showing the back-fill landed in the read slot with a changed element hash against the all-zero pristine hash `1ec64cb0955cbfc0` elsewhere). The `g3-gates` row shows the plugin's 58315-built context chain entirely null (`ctx+50=null`) — moot under criterion ① (63581 builds its own context at 0xbafe48; the 58423 gates were never reached), recorded for the v17 design. Slots-sequence facts: the two slot pointers alternate every step (Path A swap visible at steps 0→1→2) and both slots carry the pristine hash until the step-120 read slot shows the written state. Audit 15 answers the four questions v17's fix design depends on, all address-cited with reproduction commands: **Q1** — 63564 (0xbae3d0, 0x61 B) is a pure five-level non-empty chain (`[graph+0x238]` physicsWorld → `[graph+0xF0]` ragdollDriver → `driver+0x88` hkaRagdollInstance via the two-instruction pure getter 58286 → `rigidBodies[0]` → the rigid body's `hkpWorldObject::world` at +0x10) with no numeric comparison — the audit-10 blend-out/mode-byte suspect (`driver+0x10`) is structurally refuted (that offset is read only as the `rigidBodies` data pointer); on a healthy living actor the predicate returns false exactly on `rigidBodies[0]->world == 0` (ragdoll bodies not registered into a physics world), and that is the only flip that does not break the chain (the explicit field values that would flip the return are listed per exit). **Q2** — the 63589→63581 call site (0xbb2ac3) passes exactly two live arguments `(rcx = graph, edx = word[graph+0x240] u16)` with no stack arguments; every other register is dead at first use, and the plugin's `&mode_byte` argument is consumed only by the 63568 branch (audit 14's edx reading pinned into a full convention). **Q3** — 63581's own gates before the sampling block are enumerated (edx≠0; both slots' `+4>2`/`+0x38>0`; the t-pair bracket — all statically satisfied after 63587's Path A reset), the plugin could direct-call 63581 with `(rcx=candidate, edx=word[candidate+0x240])` to bypass the 63589 fork, and pre-gate side effects are bounded (stack copy, `[0x230]/[0x234]` bookkeeping, conditional 63771) with runtime magnitudes UNVERIFIED. **Q4** — the manager's real per-frame 63587 record at the 0xba3dd5 call site (inside 0xba3c50) is passed through from caller rdx and built by 0x54c130 as `{dt=xmm1, callback/args/transform=null, id=0, +0x2A=0, +0x2B=1, +0x2C=1, +0x2E=1}` — **+0x2A=0, Path B semantics** (the LOD resolver runs and 0x245/0x246 are cleared), so the engine's own per-frame sampling executes the 63581 branch; the `+0x2A=1` records (v15's and 63602's tail) are slot-5 zero-dt pipeline specials. Audit 7's "+0x2A non-zero = skip selection" reading is confirmed verbatim at 0x54c1f0's `cmp byte [rdx+0x2A], bl`. The closing factual summary names what v17 must arrange (predicate flip on `rigidBodies[0]->world` + sampling reach via Path-B-semantics b246=0, direct-call convention documented) without designing v17, and marks the 63581-built context chain values UNVERIFIED (never to be extrapolated from the null plugin-context g3-gates row). - Add the eleventh 1.6.1170 binary audit (slot-2 identity and the element-field writer) and the G2 v12 direct slot-2 call. - Add the tenth 1.6.1170 binary audit (ragdoll provider identity and fill chain) and the G2 v11 ragdoll-instance borrow. The audit retires all three audit-9 suspects (58453 is only the hkbContext build plus the `[this+0x58]` object's vtable slot 4; 63356 only walks the actor's graph array calling the 63586 activation wrapper; 0xaea12b is a cold split-off of a larger function), then resolves the chain RTTI-first via the new `ragdoll_rtti_scan.py` (type descriptor → COL → vtable; vtable slots and vtable[-1] are stored as full VAs in this image, only intra-RTTI pointers are RVAs): the bone provider 63630 reads is the **hkaRagdollInstance** at `[graph+0xF0]→hkbRagdollDriver→[+0x88]` (58286 is the pure `[rcx+0x88]` getter), and its table/count are the `rigidBodies` hkArray at instance +0x10 (`{data +0x10, size +0x18, capacity +0x1C}` — audit 9's offsets were right but one object level off; the driver's own +0x10/+0x18 hold a blend-out float and mode bytes). The fill chain is pinned: the hkbRagdollDriver constructor (58280, raw) nulls +0x88 and is created only through 58449 — called by the graph constructor 0xbad070, the loader 63585, and **63870 (0xbc44e0, the engine's full character assembly; audited once before as an undecoded `proj_63870` listing)**; 63870 creates the driver, passes it into 63949 (where the plugin's load chain stops), and — when the project carries ragdoll data (`[[setup+0x40]+0x40]+0x98 ≠ 0`) — builds a dedicated hkaRagdollInstance through 63952 (64145 construction from the project skeleton) and stores it with 58285 (`hkbRagdollDriver::SetRagdoll`, the +0x88 writer). The candidate gap is therefore exactly a missing ragdoll instance, explaining v10's pass-through-gates-but-count-zero run.
