- Supersede the v80 alignment fix with a byte-wise copy as v81 (2026-11-08): `alignas(16)` did not change code generation — the rebuilt DLL still carried `movaps [rsp]` at the same `0x53f9c`, and a third crash (01:34, 0xC0000005, same offset) confirmed it. The faulting load reads the **source** on the stack while the ring-slot stores are `movups`, so declaring alignment only made the compiler more confident about `movaps`. v81 `f7382f59cd` (`2fcb029c…23b7b7`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `68d57d1e…`, CodeView age 420) copies records volatile byte-wise via `copy_probe_record` and drops the two 16-byte static asserts. **Offline proof**: scanning `.text` for the ring pattern now finds zero `movaps`/`movups` near it (previously 2 + 6). Suites 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v80 trio. Corrected anti-pattern: `alignas` is a promise to the compiler, not a repair — for frames entered through a hand-written thunk, either make the copy unvectorisable or fix the thunk and **verify by re-disassembly**, never by a passing static_assert. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v81-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Fix a probe-record alignment crash as v80 (2026-11-08): two panel-close crashes reported by WER (`SkyrimSE.exe` `0xC0000005`, faulting module `CharacterPanel.dll`, identical offset `0x53f9c`) disassemble to `movaps xmm0, [rsp]` in the 4096-entry ring copy. At 64 bytes per record the compiler vectorises the whole-record store with `movaps`, which needs 16-byte alignment, but `ProbeRecord`'s widest member is `uintptr_t` so it only had 8 and the static `ProbeState` carried that into the array. v79's two extra `uint16` counters made the record exactly 64 bytes — flipping `movups` to `movaps` and exposing the latent defect (v78's 60-byte record never triggered it). CrashLogger wrote no `crash-*.log`; WER was the reliable source. v80 `357f3f1644` (`8a830039…d7fb67`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `9ec7ec57…`, CodeView age 417) declares `alignas(16)` and pins both invariants with `static_assert`. Offline 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v79 trio. New anti-pattern: record layout changes flip the compiler's copy instructions, so alignment must be explicit and asserted for PODs that are bulk-copied inside static or heap containers. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v80-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).

Offline 12/6 pass; deployed after backing up the v78 trio. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v79-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Implement E2-A′ bone attribution as v78 (2026-11-08): with the 58423 gate unclosable, the identity is judged by where a residual lands. The window arm caches the pinned skeleton's bone names once (`setup->animationSkeleton->bones[i].name`, 256 slots on the render thread) and the comparator classifies each failing component as foot-chain (`foot/toe/ankle/calf/thigh/knee/leg`, case-insensitive substring) or other, keeping the first four other names verbatim. Close row gains `e2a-fail-foot`/`e2a-fail-other`; `e2a-bone` rows print refuting names. Criterion: `fail-other == 0 && fail-foot > 0` supports "identity holds except foot-IK correction"; any `fail-other` refutes — and foot-chain placement alone does not prove 58423 caused it (that remains E2-B). v78 `282163ca06` (`237c110e…b636d6`, 1,745,408 bytes, PDB `dcc93695…`, CodeView age 413); offline 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v77 trio. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v78-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Close route A with a structural finding (2026-11-08 00:30, v77): the session (log 345,300 bytes `dc9bf10b…`, truncated before expiry so no close row) printed `ik=[mode:1 count:116 global:1 root:1]` on all 195 `e2a` rows. Static verification shows why: 32898 builds the update record with `mov byte [rsp+0x56],1` (ctx+0x2e) and 63602's tail with `mov dword [rsp+0x6b],0x1000101`, so `[g+0x249]` is a hard-coded 1 on both engine paths, and the engine-wide byte `0x2022530` has **no writer in the whole image** (three `cmp` reads only). The 58423 gate is therefore structurally always open once `count>0`, no posture can close it, and the `failed-no-ik == 0` criterion is vacuous — retired. The useful residue: 101 of 195 frames had `mix-failed=0` (v75: 93 of 249), a stable subset where the identity holds exactly, implying 58423 is called every frame but only rewrites bones when foot IK actually corrects. Next: **E2-A′** add a track→bone-name map and require every failing component to fall on foot-IK-affected bones; if residuals leave that set, escalate to **E2-B** and instrument 58423's input/output. - Pre-register [C-G5-E2](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2) E2-A, the synthesised-pose identity (2026-11-06, **documentation only — no implementation, no build, no run**): G5 requires comparing every contributing layer, weight, blend parameter, speed and final bone transform, so E2 is split into A (this contract), B (layer/weight enumeration via 63666/59303 and scale) and C (speed/clock ledger). Offline verification before the contract pinned the exact chain: 63582 takes `n = min(slot1.numData, slot0.numData, numAnim)` and calls `64112(count=n, A=slot1 tracks, B=slot0 tracks, out tracks, w)`, where `64112` mixes only `trans` and `rot` per 0x30-byte track — `trans = (1−w)·A + w·B`, `rot = normalize((1−w)·A + w·B′)` with `B′ = −B` when `dot<0` and an `rsqrtss`-plus-Newton normalisation (scale is not mixed; left for E2-B) — and the weight passed in is `[g+0x230]`, not 63581's internal `alpha`. The `[g+0x148]` overwrite lands in the payload while the memcpy source is the track array, so it cannot disturb poseLocal on the main path, and the fallback branch sets `[g+0x230] = 1.0f`, which makes the same identity degenerate to `slot0` without an exception. Observation needs **no new detour**: the existing 63856 entry hook already has `rcx=poseLocal`, `rdx=&g+0x160`, `r8d=count` and sees `[g+0x230]`, `[g+0x220]`, `[g+0x228]` before 63587 swaps slots. Criteria A1–A6 pre-register full-track coverage (no sampling), per-component absolute 2e-6 (relative 1e-5 above |v|>100), weight domain, and per-path reporting; stop conditions include non-finite values, `n` above capacity, invalid slot pointers, `w` outside [0,1] and any detail loss.

Four code-side causes, all fixed: the classifier matched audit32's call-instruction addresses while the callee sees the return address (call+5, as `sample-entry caller=0xba3f6e` already showed); capacity/slot/pose facts were collected for every graph although the clone and others carry an uninitialized table (436 false `count-over-capacity`/`clamped-zero` and garbage slot words); returns from threads that never held a tracked entry were miscounted as orphans (80,651); and the in-flight high-water mark was sampled after the return. v71 `0327e15fa3` (`cbeba8a2…c722f4`, 1,633,792 bytes, PDB `16a29f59…`, CodeView age 400) adds per-path `apply-completions`, `untracked-returns`, and prints `path=`/`caller=` on every apply-entry row so an unclassified value is visible next time. - AFP10 clothing on the panel figure: the driver only ever looked at the player's two hands, so the
  puppet carried weapons and nothing else. Worn BIPED body items (armor, clothes, boots, jewelry) are
  now mirrored through a shared `equipment_mirror` module - the copy route's own worn-item walk moved
  there, so "what is the player wearing" has one answer - dressed while the reference is still beside
  the player (the order the copy route validated, because a biped change is applied asynchronously)
  and then reconciled every frame while the panel is open and every 30 frames while the world runs.
  The reconciliation is stateless: both inventories are read fresh, so a missed frame cannot
  desynchronise it and nothing survives a 3D generation change. Weapons stay with the hand poll.

- AFP10 the player's body shape on the puppet. OBody NG hands a freshly placed NPC a *random* preset
  every session (its log: `No preset defined for this actor, getting it randomly` -> `Preset Mia UBE
  will be applied to CharacterPanel Puppet`, a different one next session), which is why the panel
  figure's body never matched. Shape here is mesh morphs, not node scaling - all 639 `.osp` slider
  sets on this machine carry zero bone/scale definitions and BodySlide presets are realized as `.tri`
  vertex deltas - so the copy uses the same route OBody writes with: RaceMenu skee64's
  `IBodyMorphInterface`, reached through SKSE's interface exchange (`BodyMorph`, runtime version 5).
  The module mirrors that interface's vtable order locally, checks the reported version before the
  first call and refuses an older layout rather than guessing slots. The sync compares the source's
  morphs against the target's and only writes when they disagree, clearing the target's OBody keys
  first so the random preset cannot survive the copy, then re-applying the vertex buffers in the same
  call. It runs at dressing time and is re-checked on a slow panel cadence, so OBody re-shaping the
  shell or the player changing their own body is repaired rather than assumed away.

### Fixed

- AFP9j interactable shell beside the player: `Actor::SetPosition`'s second parameter is
  `a_updateCharController`, and passing false moved only the reference - the collision and activation
  target stayed beside the player (perceptible through the activation prompt while the figure itself
  was invisible), and the engine kept deriving the 3D from the controller's stale position, which is
  the root of the earlier "world and local writes are reverted every frame" finding. The park now
  requests the warp when the reference actually moves (`park-3d warp=1`), false otherwise so the
  per-frame re-assertion stays cheap. The historical route parked this way and measured interaction as
  unreachable.

### Fixed

- AFP9i intermittent "only the axe" frame: `NiSkinInstance::frameID` is the engine's "skin already
  computed for this render frame" gate, and when the engine had computed it BEFORE the studio
  placement and the applied pose were written, `SetupGeometry` kept those matrices - the skinned body
  was drawn at the older (parked) bone worlds and vanished while a rigid attachment like a weapon
  followed its node and stayed visible. The readback images show a complete, correctly framed figure
  and a lone axe alternating within one session. The batch now invalidates that id for the tree
  (`invalidate_skin_cache`, nothing written to `boneMatrices`) so the engine recomputes from the bone
  worlds this frame wrote - which also removes the one-frame lag on an applied pose.

### Fixed

- AFP9h framing reference: the studio placement was centred against `figure_skinned_bound`, which
  reads geometry `worldBound`s - a cache the engine refreshes on its own schedule, measured still
  carrying the pre-park world position while the bones had already moved to the anchor. Centring on
  that stale centre threw the figure ~2820 world units deeper than the anchor, which is why the first
  readback image showed it small, dark (far from the studio rig) and off-centre while `center-d`
  reported 0. Framing now measures `figure_skeleton_bound` (live bone world positions, palette slots
  filtered through the tree's own node set) and logs both bounds side by side. The park is also
  re-asserted on paused frames unless this frame's framing owns the transform, because the menu
  renders the world behind it and the engine's per-frame derivation otherwise puts the clone back
  beside the player the moment the panel opens.

### Added

Deployed v85 `e5552e89…`, dormant INI `27f481f5…`, branch synced through `2dea193`. The three open design questions for the E3 implementation review: manifest index scheme vs the instrument mutual-exclusion discipline, the time-axis alignment between source settled snapshots and candidate step sequences, and the dual foot-IK call-sequence anchors. [Summary](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#status-20261108-session).
- Pre-register [C-G5-E3](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3), the natural-source pairing contract (2026-11-08, **documentation only**): E3 must produce a qualified same-generation source/candidate pair so the pose comparison has a credible reference. E2-B's finding carries over as a constraint — the out-block track count takes two values (116/98), so the manifest must record both sides' foot-IK call counts and scopes, and source/candidate are inherently cross-thread so pairing follows the manifest's input/time axis rather than threads. Four deliverables: C1 source qualification (fixed save/equipment/skeleton/OAR config, natural progression, control runs separating natural evolution from candidate interference), C2 bound-resource identity including OAR replacement/variants (never just animationName or the shared binding set), C3 the pairing manifest fixed **before** any pose-error look with quantisation and ambiguity rules (v66's unqualified `12220/SVFI/13.06` vs `1022/original/1.8667` pair registered as the counter-example), and C4 full same-generation records plus a missing-data ledger where candidate defects are measured differences rather than dropped frames. R1 stays diagnostic-only; no candidate hash repetition substitutes for a source oracle. Offline pre-check: both sides' recorders exist (SourceTimeline, the E2 probes); the gap is a shared manifest index (epoch/request/view plus both foot-IK call sequences). No implementation authorised; gate 1 stays open until the joint acceptance. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3).

- Implement the E2-B foot-IK observation as v83 (2026-11-08): two new detours on 58423 — entry (7-byte chain-addressing head) and return (the single `ret`'s tail window, both verified offline for interior jump targets). The entry recorder snapshots the out block through the policy reader `probe_read_footik_out` and registers a per-tid in-flight entry; the return recorder re-reads, compares component-wise with the E2-A thresholds and pushes `b-entry`/`b-return` pairs. - Converge E2-A: every residual lands on one of three enumerable post-process chains (2026-11-08, v82 run, log 404,515 bytes `c6447fe0…`): 436 fallback frames, `e2a-failed=10,421` of 354,032 components (2.94%), `fail-frames=312/436`, `fail-foot=5,921`, `fail-cloth=626`, `fail-unexplained=3,874` — and the eight aggregated bone slots are all **arm chain and attachment** (`NPC L/R Forearm`, `R UpperArm`, `L/R Hand`, `AnimObjectL`, `Shield`, `L Pauldron`), i.e. hand IK plus equipment attachment, a third post-process class beside foot IK and cloth. Sample deltas are 3.2e-6..7.6e-6 (relative 1e-5..5e-5), above plain lerp rounding, so by the pre-registered bounds they are genuine failures and were **not** relaxed away. A separate threshold-argument fix (v83, committed but **not built or deployed**) changes the rule to "absolute OR relative, whichever is looser" because relative error is scale-free; it does not absorb those samples, so the reading stands. **E2-A conclusion**: the identity is exact off the post-process chains (148/223 frames component-wise equal in v81) but a strict zero-residual identity is unreachable by design, since 63581's memcpy consumes an `out` block already processed by 58423 (foot IK), with hand IK and attachment rewriting bones as well; the criterion should become "residuals ⊆ enumerable post-process bone set". Next: E2-B, hooking 58423 (and locating the hand-IK/attachment entries) to observe post-process input/output explicitly. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2).
- Split E2-A′ residuals into foot, cloth and unexplained buckets as v82 (2026-11-08): the v81 run (log 406,632 bytes `7c6f2353…`) completed without crashing and gave the first full reading — 148 of 223 visible frames matched exactly, `e2a-failed=3,565` of 234,668 components (1.52%), `fail-frames=86`, `fail-foot=1,086`, `fail-other=2,479` with **all four sample slots on `SkirtRBone01`**, and `near-slot0=2,529` (boundary errors: the sample's `|v|=6.6` has `rel≈1e-6` while the pre-registered rule applies `abs 2e-6` below 100). Every "other" residual was therefore a **cloth** bone, not an unexplained one, so the foot-IK-only split was too narrow and the fixed sample table hid the real set. v82 `36cc25bbca` (`229547fe…18505dd`, 1,780,224 bytes, PDB `45c7be66…`, CodeView age 422) classifies foot/cloth/unexplained, aggregates unexplained names by bone with counts (up to 8), and replaces `e2a-fail-other` with `e2a-fail-cloth`/`e2a-fail-unexplained`. Criterion: `fail-unexplained == 0`. Offline 13/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v81 trio. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v82-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Supersede the v80 alignment fix with a byte-wise copy as v81 (2026-11-08): `alignas(16)` did not change code generation — the rebuilt DLL still carried `movaps [rsp]` at the same `0x53f9c`, and a third crash (01:34, 0xC0000005, same offset) confirmed it. The faulting load reads the **source** on the stack while the ring-slot stores are `movups`, so declaring alignment only made the compiler more confident about `movaps`. v81 `f7382f59cd` (`2fcb029c…23b7b7`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `68d57d1e…`, CodeView age 420) copies records volatile byte-wise via `copy_probe_record` and drops the two 16-byte static asserts. **Offline proof**: scanning `.text` for the ring pattern now finds zero `movaps`/`movups` near it (previously 2 + 6). Suites 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v80 trio. Corrected anti-pattern: `alignas` is a promise to the compiler, not a repair — for frames entered through a hand-written thunk, either make the copy unvectorisable or fix the thunk and **verify by re-disassembly**, never by a passing static_assert. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v81-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Fix a probe-record alignment crash as v80 (2026-11-08): two panel-close crashes reported by WER (`SkyrimSE.exe` `0xC0000005`, faulting module `CharacterPanel.dll`, identical offset `0x53f9c`) disassemble to `movaps xmm0, [rsp]` in the 4096-entry ring copy. At 64 bytes per record the compiler vectorises the whole-record store with `movaps`, which needs 16-byte alignment, but `ProbeRecord`'s widest member is `uintptr_t` so it only had 8 and the static `ProbeState` carried that into the array. v79's two extra `uint16` counters made the record exactly 64 bytes — flipping `movups` to `movaps` and exposing the latent defect (v78's 60-byte record never triggered it). CrashLogger wrote no `crash-*.log`; WER was the reliable source. v80 `357f3f1644` (`8a830039…d7fb67`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `9ec7ec57…`, CodeView age 417) declares `alignas(16)` and pins both invariants with `static_assert`. Offline 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v79 trio. New anti-pattern: record layout changes flip the compiler's copy instructions, so alignment must be explicit and asserted for PODs that are bulk-copied inside static or heap containers. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v80-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).

- Implement E2-A′ bone attribution as v78 (2026-11-08): with the 58423 gate unclosable, the identity is judged by where a residual lands. The window arm caches the pinned skeleton's bone names once (`setup->animationSkeleton->bones[i].name`, 256 slots on the render thread) and the comparator classifies each failing component as foot-chain (`foot/toe/ankle/calf/thigh/knee/leg`, case-insensitive substring) or other, keeping the first four other names verbatim. Close row gains `e2a-fail-foot`/`e2a-fail-other`; `e2a-bone` rows print refuting names. Criterion: `fail-other == 0 && fail-foot > 0` supports "identity holds except foot-IK correction"; any `fail-other` refutes — and foot-chain placement alone does not prove 58423 caused it (that remains E2-B). v78 `282163ca06` (`237c110e…b636d6`, 1,745,408 bytes, PDB `dcc93695…`, CodeView age 413); offline 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v77 trio. - Close route A with a structural finding (2026-11-08 00:30, v77): the session (log 345,300 bytes `dc9bf10b…`, truncated before expiry so no close row) printed `ik=[mode:1 count:116 global:1 root:1]` on all 195 `e2a` rows. Static verification shows why: 32898 builds the update record with `mov byte [rsp+0x56],1` (ctx+0x2e) and 63602's tail with `mov dword [rsp+0x6b],0x1000101`, so `[g+0x249]` is a hard-coded 1 on both engine paths, and the engine-wide byte `0x2022530` has **no writer in the whole image** (three `cmp` reads only). The 58423 gate is therefore structurally always open once `count>0`, no posture can close it, and the `failed-no-ik == 0` criterion is vacuous — retired. The useful residue: 101 of 195 frames had `mix-failed=0` (v75: 93 of 249), a stable subset where the identity holds exactly, implying 58423 is called every frame but only rewrites bones when foot IK actually corrects. Next: **E2-A′** add a track→bone-name map and require every failing component to fall on foot-IK-affected bones; if residuals leave that set, escalate to **E2-B** and instrument 58423's input/output. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v77-g5e2-a-2026-11-07).

It was open on all 354 v75 frames, so `fail-ik == fail-frames` could not separate anything and the §十二 "residual = IK post-process" attribution is **withdrawn**; only the raw readings survive (9,223 mismatches, 21–26 per frame, `max-delta` ≈ 0.1795, track-6 samples). The real 58423 gate at 0xbb0040..0xbb0076 is `word[g+0x240]>0 && byte[g+0x249]!=0 && byte[0x2022530]!=0 && [g+0x218]!=nullptr`; `byte[g+0x249]` is rewritten per frame by 63587 (ctx+0x2e at 0xbb23b7 / frame state at 0xbb28da) and `0x2022530` is an engine-wide byte. v77 `236775890a` (`ac96f2a4…262ca`, 1,742,336 bytes, PDB `c5757848…`, CodeView age 411) adds `probe_ik_gate_open` as a pure function, prints all four input bytes on every `e2a` row and splits failures by the corrected gate. - Implement the E2-A synthesised-pose identity as v72 (2026-11-06, **built and deployed, awaiting the run**): `probe_liveness_policy.h` gains the 64112 mix as pure functions — `probe_mix_trans` with the engine's `(1−w)·A + w·B` association, `probe_mix_rot` with the sign correction and zero-length convention, `probe_mix_delta` (absolute 2e-6, relative 1e-5 above 100 units), `probe_slot_view` (container → slot → tracks via the signed +0x34 offset behind the `dword[+4] > 2` gate) and `probe_compare_track` (translation channels 0–2, rotation 4–7) — plus a `ProbeMixTally` holding frames, tracks, failures, worst delta and four diagnostic samples. The existing 63856 entry recorder now reads `[g+0x230]`, both slot track arrays and poseLocal for the pinned graph, compares every track without sampling, and pushes one `k_mix_frame` record per frame; the render drain prints `e2a` rows and the close row gains `e2a-frames/tracks/failed/max-delta/ok/w-domain/non-finite/read-failed/samples`. No new detour, no native writes, no new INI key. Offline: seven E2-A cases pass (endpoints, sign correction, zero length, the 100-unit tolerance switch, tally and sample cap, an explicit hand-computed identity with two single-channel corruptions, slot-view plausibility) alongside v3 (6), repair (2) and legacy (8). Identity `CharacterPanel-scenegraph-v72-g5e2-a-mix-identity-5dd64a60d0-cl94faaed0c6-20261106T160252Z`, DLL 1,704,960 bytes SHA-256 `bb27a87f…538577`, PDB `7d809017…`, CodeView age 401; deployed with the concurrent INI after backing up the v71 trio. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v72-g5e2-a-2026-11-06).
- Pre-register [C-G5-E2](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2) E2-A, the synthesised-pose identity (2026-11-06, **documentation only — no implementation, no build, no run**): G5 requires comparing every contributing layer, weight, blend parameter, speed and final bone transform, so E2 is split into A (this contract), B (layer/weight enumeration via 63666/59303 and scale) and C (speed/clock ledger). Offline verification before the contract pinned the exact chain: 63582 takes `n = min(slot1.numData, slot0.numData, numAnim)` and calls `64112(count=n, A=slot1 tracks, B=slot0 tracks, out tracks, w)`, where `64112` mixes only `trans` and `rot` per 0x30-byte track — `trans = (1−w)·A + w·B`, `rot = normalize((1−w)·A + w·B′)` with `B′ = −B` when `dot<0` and an `rsqrtss`-plus-Newton normalisation (scale is not mixed; left for E2-B) — and the weight passed in is `[g+0x230]`, not 63581's internal `alpha`. The `[g+0x148]` overwrite lands in the payload while the memcpy source is the track array, so it cannot disturb poseLocal on the main path, and the fallback branch sets `[g+0x230] = 1.0f`, which makes the same identity degenerate to `slot0` without an exception. Observation needs **no new detour**: the existing 63856 entry hook already has `rcx=poseLocal`, `rdx=&g+0x160`, `r8d=count` and sees `[g+0x230]`, `[g+0x220]`, `[g+0x228]` before 63587 swaps slots. Criteria A1–A6 pre-register full-track coverage (no sampling), per-component absolute 2e-6 (relative 1e-5 above |v|>100), weight domain, and per-path reporting; stop conditions include non-finite values, `n` above capacity, invalid slot pointers, `w` outside [0,1] and any detail loss.

Close row: `apply-paths=63589:1356/63568:77481/p63563:10600/p63572:0/p_bae731:0/un:0` summing exactly to `apply-entries=89,437` with **`unclassified=0`**, `apply-completions=63568:424` equal to both `completions-pinned` and `pinned-apply-entries` (424/424 same-thread pairs, `in-flight-now=0`, `in-flight-max=1`), `count-over-capacity=0`, `clamped-zero=0`, `pose-stable=yes`, `fill-le-cap=ok`, `skeleton=[cap:116 fill:116]`, `transport-complete=yes`, and `orphan-returns=65,195` explained as non-pinned returns on tracked threads (`untracked-returns=23,818`). **R1–R7 all satisfied**: the generation-path completion boundary is proven for the pinned source graph, five-path classification reconciles, the main-path range shows no over-capacity, the pose pointer is stable, fill never exceeds capacity, the in-flight window is quantified at one, and no cross-epoch pairing appeared. First running evidence for the physics-callback path (`p63563=10,600`). Limits recorded: only the pinned graph's generation path is covered, the `slot-num-data` field reads implausible values and is excluded from every criterion, and orphan/untracked returns are not lost pairs. Gate 1 stays NOT_READY (E2/E3 and joint acceptance remain). INI restored to dormant `27f481f5…`. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e1-v3), [run record](docs/build.md#v71-g5e1-v3-fix1-2026-11-06).
- Adjudicate the first v3 run and fix four instrument defects as v71 (2026-11-06): the v70 two-segment window (log 377,591 bytes `e07e71e8…`) installed all three hooks including the new return site and produced strong completion evidence — `apply-returns = apply-entries = 81,034` (every 63856 call returned through the hook) and `completions-pinned = pinned-apply-entries = 383` (the pinned source graph paired 383/383 on the same thread), with `pose-stable=yes`, `fill-le-cap=ok`, `skeleton=[cap:116 fill:116]` and zero transport gaps — but `apply-paths` was all-unclassified (81,034), hitting the R2 hard stop, so no criterion passes. Four code-side causes, all fixed: the classifier matched audit32's call-instruction addresses while the callee sees the return address (call+5, as `sample-entry caller=0xba3f6e` already showed); capacity/slot/pose facts were collected for every graph although the clone and others carry an uninitialized table (436 false `count-over-capacity`/`clamped-zero` and garbage slot words); returns from threads that never held a tracked entry were miscounted as orphans (80,651); and the in-flight high-water mark was sampled after the return. v71 `0327e15fa3` (`cbeba8a2…c722f4`, 1,633,792 bytes, PDB `16a29f59…`, CodeView age 400) adds per-path `apply-completions`, `untracked-returns`, and prints `path=`/`caller=` on every apply-entry row so an unclassified value is visible next time. Six+two+eight engine-free cases pass; built, not deployed. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e1-v3), [build](docs/build.md#v71-g5e1-v3-fix1-2026-11-06).
- AFP9g forensics: the studio target can be read back to a TGA under `.../SKSE/CharacterPanel/`
  (`r01-panel-NNN.tga`, first drawn frame and every 300 after, capped) - the facility this project
  used before 172984a and dropped, which turned every later round into an external frame-capture
  conversation. Alongside it: an `R01 census` line (geometry/skinned counts, and the first skinned
  geometry's skin `frameID`/`num-matrices`/first-bone world and bound) and a `park-3d ref-now`
  read-back that settles whether `SetPosition` moves the reference at all. The park is now re-asserted
  every unpaused frame instead of every 60, because the engine re-derives the transform each frame and
  wins over a one-shot write.

### Fixed

- AFP9f transform layer: a live actor's subtree is re-derived from its root's `local` every frame, so
  a world-space write lasts one frame (measured: the park's world write was undone 1.3 s later, which
  is why the clone stayed beside the player), and the skin state is computed during the graph update,
  so a placement applied afterwards reaches rigid attachments only (the axe followed, the skinned body
  did not). Placement now writes the root's `local` together with the derived worlds, and R01 applies
  it in `begin_frame` BEFORE the puppet's graph update, restoring it in `end_frame` after the draw
  branch on every path.

- AFP9e visible clone beside the player: `PuppetDriver::park()` moved only the reference position, and
  this actor's 3D is never re-derived from it (measured: twenty unpaused seconds with the reference
  parked while the root's world stayed at the player), so restoring the fade left a fully visible
  clone standing next to the player - and it is also why the F6 figure projected arbitrarily, since
  the tree never left the player. The park now moves the 3D subtree by the same world offset,
  computed against the absolute target so repeated parks cannot stack, and logs
  `park-3d ref-t / world-t-before / world-t-after / moved-z` as the reconciliation line.

- AFP9c F6 off-screen figure: the R01 framing wrote the puppet root's `local` and cascaded, but a
  downward pass recomputes the children from the object's own world and never moves the object - so
  the figure stayed at its park depth (`restore world-d=(0,0,0)`) and every vertex projected far
  outside the frustum (NDC ≈ (236, 290), the user's RenderDoc reading). The framing is now a
  world-space delta applied downward (`figure_world_delta` reusing the copy route's own solve plus
  `apply_world_delta_downward`), which touches no local at all, and it asserts itself with a
  `SCOPY R01 frame … center-d=` line. The measured bound is refreshed before framing, the root's
  bound cache is written back on exit, and a unit case covers the delta.

- AFP9b black panel: the figure draw and the composite shared one D3D state-capture scope, so the
  composite bound the studio texture as a sampler source while it was still bound as the output
  target - D3D11 silently unbinds a resource used as both, and the panel sampled nothing while the
  studio target itself was correct (both routes affected; the user's RenderDoc reading was right).
  The composite now releases the output target before binding its source, the draw and the composite
  use separate capture scopes again, and the composite reads the bound source back once and logs
  `SCOPY COMPOSITE src/bound/match`. The audit's unreachable naming was also wrong (a negative bone
  table target is not unreachable - the name bridge writes those slots; 61 names were printed against
  a per-frame count of 6) and now repeats the write's own resolution order with a `reconciles=` check;
  the R01 restore check no longer fails on the worldBound cache the engine refreshes during pass
  setup (world drift was already 0), which also removes its per-frame log flood.

- AFP9 first-test crash (`EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION` at `CharacterPanel.dll+0x2B78A`, a name read at
  `0x1010`): the coverage audit's palette walk dereferenced `NiSkinInstance::bones` entries as nodes
  while reading their names. The walk now collects pointers only - the shape the probe has run for
  weeks - and a slot is classified through the node the caller resolves itself in the actor's tree,
  which also reports whether the name resolved at all (`resolved=x/y`). The probe's private palette
  walk was deleted in favour of the shared one, so the memory rule exists once. Field logs are kept
  in `docs/diagnostics`; verdict in `docs/afp9-verdict-2026-10-13.md`.

### Added

- Record the session summary and current state (2026-11-08, documentation): E2 fully closed (E2-A′ identity convergence attributing every residual to the three enumerable post-process chains; E2-B observing foot IK as the dominant poseLocal writer at ~2.7 calls/frame and ~184 components rewritten per call, plus the new 116/98 out-track dual values), two crash fixes (v81's volatile byte-wise ring copy; v84's out-pointer dereference), two new anti-patterns (alignas is a promise not a repair — verify by re-disassembly; hook argument layouts must be derived from the consumer side), E3 pre-registered with its four deliverables and the manifest-index gap, and gate-1 progress E1 ✅ / E2 ✅ / E3 contract ready / joint acceptance pending. Deployed v85 `e5552e89…`, dormant INI `27f481f5…`, branch synced through `2dea193`. The three open design questions for the E3 implementation review: manifest index scheme vs the instrument mutual-exclusion discipline, the time-axis alignment between source settled snapshots and candidate step sequences, and the dual foot-IK call-sequence anchors. [Summary](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#status-20261108-session).
- Pre-register [C-G5-E3](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e3), the natural-source pairing contract (2026-11-08, **documentation only**): E3 must produce a qualified same-generation source/candidate pair so the pose comparison has a credible reference. E2-B's finding carries over as a constraint — the out-block track count takes two values (116/98), so the manifest must record both sides' foot-IK call counts and scopes, and source/candidate are inherently cross-thread so pairing follows the manifest's input/time axis rather than threads. - Supersede the v80 alignment fix with a byte-wise copy as v81 (2026-11-08): `alignas(16)` did not change code generation — the rebuilt DLL still carried `movaps [rsp]` at the same `0x53f9c`, and a third crash (01:34, 0xC0000005, same offset) confirmed it. The faulting load reads the **source** on the stack while the ring-slot stores are `movups`, so declaring alignment only made the compiler more confident about `movaps`. v81 `f7382f59cd` (`2fcb029c…23b7b7`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `68d57d1e…`, CodeView age 420) copies records volatile byte-wise via `copy_probe_record` and drops the two 16-byte static asserts. **Offline proof**: scanning `.text` for the ring pattern now finds zero `movaps`/`movups` near it (previously 2 + 6). Suites 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v80 trio. Corrected anti-pattern: `alignas` is a promise to the compiler, not a repair — for frames entered through a hand-written thunk, either make the copy unvectorisable or fix the thunk and **verify by re-disassembly**, never by a passing static_assert. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v81-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Fix a probe-record alignment crash as v80 (2026-11-08): two panel-close crashes reported by WER (`SkyrimSE.exe` `0xC0000005`, faulting module `CharacterPanel.dll`, identical offset `0x53f9c`) disassemble to `movaps xmm0, [rsp]` in the 4096-entry ring copy. At 64 bytes per record the compiler vectorises the whole-record store with `movaps`, which needs 16-byte alignment, but `ProbeRecord`'s widest member is `uintptr_t` so it only had 8 and the static `ProbeState` carried that into the array. v79's two extra `uint16` counters made the record exactly 64 bytes — flipping `movups` to `movaps` and exposing the latent defect (v78's 60-byte record never triggered it). CrashLogger wrote no `crash-*.log`; WER was the reliable source. v80 `357f3f1644` (`8a830039…d7fb67`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `9ec7ec57…`, CodeView age 417) declares `alignas(16)` and pins both invariants with `static_assert`. Offline 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v79 trio. New anti-pattern: record layout changes flip the compiler's copy instructions, so alignment must be explicit and asserted for PODs that are bulk-copied inside static or heap containers. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v80-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).

- Close route A with a structural finding (2026-11-08 00:30, v77): the session (log 345,300 bytes `dc9bf10b…`, truncated before expiry so no close row) printed `ik=[mode:1 count:116 global:1 root:1]` on all 195 `e2a` rows. Static verification shows why: 32898 builds the update record with `mov byte [rsp+0x56],1` (ctx+0x2e) and 63602's tail with `mov dword [rsp+0x6b],0x1000101`, so `[g+0x249]` is a hard-coded 1 on both engine paths, and the engine-wide byte `0x2022530` has **no writer in the whole image** (three `cmp` reads only). The 58423 gate is therefore structurally always open once `count>0`, no posture can close it, and the `failed-no-ik == 0` criterion is vacuous — retired. The useful residue: 101 of 195 frames had `mix-failed=0` (v75: 93 of 249), a stable subset where the identity holds exactly, implying 58423 is called every frame but only rewrites bones when foot IK actually corrects. - Pre-register [C-G5-E2](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2) E2-A, the synthesised-pose identity (2026-11-06, **documentation only — no implementation, no build, no run**): G5 requires comparing every contributing layer, weight, blend parameter, speed and final bone transform, so E2 is split into A (this contract), B (layer/weight enumeration via 63666/59303 and scale) and C (speed/clock ledger). Offline verification before the contract pinned the exact chain: 63582 takes `n = min(slot1.numData, slot0.numData, numAnim)` and calls `64112(count=n, A=slot1 tracks, B=slot0 tracks, out tracks, w)`, where `64112` mixes only `trans` and `rot` per 0x30-byte track — `trans = (1−w)·A + w·B`, `rot = normalize((1−w)·A + w·B′)` with `B′ = −B` when `dot<0` and an `rsqrtss`-plus-Newton normalisation (scale is not mixed; left for E2-B) — and the weight passed in is `[g+0x230]`, not 63581's internal `alpha`. The `[g+0x148]` overwrite lands in the payload while the memcpy source is the track array, so it cannot disturb poseLocal on the main path, and the fallback branch sets `[g+0x230] = 1.0f`, which makes the same identity degenerate to `slot0` without an exception. Observation needs **no new detour**: the existing 63856 entry hook already has `rcx=poseLocal`, `rdx=&g+0x160`, `r8d=count` and sees `[g+0x230]`, `[g+0x220]`, `[g+0x228]` before 63587 swaps slots. Criteria A1–A6 pre-register full-track coverage (no sampling), per-component absolute 2e-6 (relative 1e-5 above |v|>100), weight domain, and per-path reporting; stop conditions include non-finite values, `n` above capacity, invalid slot pointers, `w` outside [0,1] and any detail loss.

- AFP9 package A: the application stage reconciles its coverage ledger every frame
  (output-slots = attempted + written + unreachable + unprocessed, the A2 mark carries it) and, when
  the gap changes, names the unreachable and unprocessed slots against the animation skeleton and
  classifies each by the puppet's own skin palette (necessary / helper / weapon-attachment; an
  unbound palette reports `unbound-palette`, never "nothing is necessary"). The audit names and
  classifies only - it does not extend the write past the mapping table, because the `Weapon*`
  slots AFP8 measured as graph-driven residue would move the weapons if written blindly.
- AFP9 package B, R01: an opt-in (F6) same-Actor direct-draw path. When enabled the panel draws the
  puppet's own `Get3D(false)` tree through the shared pass seam instead of the detached copy, with a
  provenance line (actor form, root, graph, generation), fixed-bone A4 (pre-pass) and A5 (post-draw)
  samples proving the draw does not overwrite the applied pose, and a temporary framing whose saved
  root local, world and worldBound are restored with a printed before/after reconciliation on every
  exit including failures. The puppet path falls back to the copy and logs the reason; physics and
  FaceGen stay out of scope (A3 = skipped).
- Shared audit helpers (`figure_audit.h/.cpp`): skin-palette names, collision-helper-filtered
  skinned bound, placement solve, and the slot-necessity classifier, unit-tested alongside the new
  coverage-ledger arithmetic in the focused regression target.

- Replace exploratory verification matrices with two focused regressions for snapshot placement
  and final-pose index-order calibration.

- Supersede the v80 alignment fix with a byte-wise copy as v81 (2026-11-08): `alignas(16)` did not change code generation — the rebuilt DLL still carried `movaps [rsp]` at the same `0x53f9c`, and a third crash (01:34, 0xC0000005, same offset) confirmed it. The faulting load reads the **source** on the stack while the ring-slot stores are `movups`, so declaring alignment only made the compiler more confident about `movaps`. v81 `f7382f59cd` (`2fcb029c…23b7b7`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `68d57d1e…`, CodeView age 420) copies records volatile byte-wise via `copy_probe_record` and drops the two 16-byte static asserts. **Offline proof**: scanning `.text` for the ring pattern now finds zero `movaps`/`movups` near it (previously 2 + 6). Suites 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v80 trio. Corrected anti-pattern: `alignas` is a promise to the compiler, not a repair — for frames entered through a hand-written thunk, either make the copy unvectorisable or fix the thunk and **verify by re-disassembly**, never by a passing static_assert. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v81-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Fix a probe-record alignment crash as v80 (2026-11-08): two panel-close crashes reported by WER (`SkyrimSE.exe` `0xC0000005`, faulting module `CharacterPanel.dll`, identical offset `0x53f9c`) disassemble to `movaps xmm0, [rsp]` in the 4096-entry ring copy. At 64 bytes per record the compiler vectorises the whole-record store with `movaps`, which needs 16-byte alignment, but `ProbeRecord`'s widest member is `uintptr_t` so it only had 8 and the static `ProbeState` carried that into the array. v79's two extra `uint16` counters made the record exactly 64 bytes — flipping `movups` to `movaps` and exposing the latent defect (v78's 60-byte record never triggered it). CrashLogger wrote no `crash-*.log`; WER was the reliable source. v80 `357f3f1644` (`8a830039…d7fb67`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `9ec7ec57…`, CodeView age 417) declares `alignas(16)` and pins both invariants with `static_assert`. Offline 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v79 trio. New anti-pattern: record layout changes flip the compiler's copy instructions, so alignment must be explicit and asserted for PODs that are bulk-copied inside static or heap containers. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v80-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).

- Close route A with a structural finding (2026-11-08 00:30, v77): the session (log 345,300 bytes `dc9bf10b…`, truncated before expiry so no close row) printed `ik=[mode:1 count:116 global:1 root:1]` on all 195 `e2a` rows. Static verification shows why: 32898 builds the update record with `mov byte [rsp+0x56],1` (ctx+0x2e) and 63602's tail with `mov dword [rsp+0x6b],0x1000101`, so `[g+0x249]` is a hard-coded 1 on both engine paths, and the engine-wide byte `0x2022530` has **no writer in the whole image** (three `cmp` reads only). The 58423 gate is therefore structurally always open once `count>0`, no posture can close it, and the `failed-no-ik == 0` criterion is vacuous — retired. The useful residue: 101 of 195 frames had `mix-failed=0` (v75: 93 of 249), a stable subset where the identity holds exactly, implying 58423 is called every frame but only rewrites bones when foot IK actually corrects. Next: **E2-A′** add a track→bone-name map and require every failing component to fall on foot-IK-affected bones; if residuals leave that set, escalate to **E2-B** and instrument 58423's input/output. - Implement the E2-A synthesised-pose identity as v72 (2026-11-06, **built and deployed, awaiting the run**): `probe_liveness_policy.h` gains the 64112 mix as pure functions — `probe_mix_trans` with the engine's `(1−w)·A + w·B` association, `probe_mix_rot` with the sign correction and zero-length convention, `probe_mix_delta` (absolute 2e-6, relative 1e-5 above 100 units), `probe_slot_view` (container → slot → tracks via the signed +0x34 offset behind the `dword[+4] > 2` gate) and `probe_compare_track` (translation channels 0–2, rotation 4–7) — plus a `ProbeMixTally` holding frames, tracks, failures, worst delta and four diagnostic samples. The existing 63856 entry recorder now reads `[g+0x230]`, both slot track arrays and poseLocal for the pinned graph, compares every track without sampling, and pushes one `k_mix_frame` record per frame; the render drain prints `e2a` rows and the close row gains `e2a-frames/tracks/failed/max-delta/ok/w-domain/non-finite/read-failed/samples`. - Pre-register [C-G5-E2](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2) E2-A, the synthesised-pose identity (2026-11-06, **documentation only — no implementation, no build, no run**): G5 requires comparing every contributing layer, weight, blend parameter, speed and final bone transform, so E2 is split into A (this contract), B (layer/weight enumeration via 63666/59303 and scale) and C (speed/clock ledger). Offline verification before the contract pinned the exact chain: 63582 takes `n = min(slot1.numData, slot0.numData, numAnim)` and calls `64112(count=n, A=slot1 tracks, B=slot0 tracks, out tracks, w)`, where `64112` mixes only `trans` and `rot` per 0x30-byte track — `trans = (1−w)·A + w·B`, `rot = normalize((1−w)·A + w·B′)` with `B′ = −B` when `dot<0` and an `rsqrtss`-plus-Newton normalisation (scale is not mixed; left for E2-B) — and the weight passed in is `[g+0x230]`, not 63581's internal `alpha`. The `[g+0x148]` overwrite lands in the payload while the memcpy source is the track array, so it cannot disturb poseLocal on the main path, and the fallback branch sets `[g+0x230] = 1.0f`, which makes the same identity degenerate to `slot0` without an exception. Observation needs **no new detour**: the existing 63856 entry hook already has `rcx=poseLocal`, `rdx=&g+0x160`, `r8d=count` and sees `[g+0x230]`, `[g+0x220]`, `[g+0x228]` before 63587 swaps slots. Criteria A1–A6 pre-register full-track coverage (no sampling), per-component absolute 2e-6 (relative 1e-5 above |v|>100), weight domain, and per-path reporting; stop conditions include non-finite values, `n` above capacity, invalid slot pointers, `w` outside [0,1] and any detail loss.

- Implement the same-Actor route's application stage: the behaviour graph's own final output pose
  written into the puppet's skeleton, which is the half the engine does not do while the panel owns
  the clock (HKX42 measured the locals frozen). It is deliberately strict. It writes only a mapping
  it has just verified against the engine's own applied node locals through the same scoring and
  decision the forensics print, and blocks with the reason when that fails - so verification happens
  while the world is RUNNING (the engine applies the pose then, which makes its locals the reference)
  and the write happens while the panel pauses the world; arming it while already paused reports
  `unverified-world-first` instead of guessing, and it never re-scores once it has started writing,
  because the locals would then be its own. Every node is resolved by name in the current Actor tree
  on the frame it writes, with the engine's bone table as a fallback; no node pointer is kept across
  frames. The write sets
  local transforms and recomputes the subtree's worlds once, the only path this codebase has measured
  reaching a draw. A2 reports what it did on every mark: the mapping, the generation, how many bones
  the last frame posed, how many pose indices the chosen mapping could not reach, and the worst change
  it made. F4's cycle carries `tick+apply` and `tick+apply+force`, with `step` as one declared dt plus one application,
  and the driver gains the single step that runs even while its gate is closed.

### Changed

- F6 is no longer documented as "gone": AFP9 binds R01 direct draw to it. F3 remains with the other
  mod that owns it. The package README's control table and the maintenance document carry the same
  contract.
- Move the AFP route's controls onto the available keys: F2 marks and F4 is
  the drive cycle; placement has no control entry. The earlier bindings were wrong or unwanted - F5 is the
  game's own quicksave (and this sink does not consume input), F11/F12 and NUMPAD1 were tried and are
  not wanted - and the scene-copy route gives up its old F2 idle / F3 rotate / F4 release bindings for
  them, keeping F7 (re-capture, which parks the previous copy) and F8 (draw it). F4 is designed to
  absorb the application stage rather than to need a fourth key: it becomes off -> tick -> tick+apply
  -> tick+apply+force -> step-once, which is R02's push/freeze control, R04's A/B pair and single-step on one key. The
  control contract now also states what deliberately has no key - birth, display and equipment
  mirroring are automatic, framing is a build-time preset, and physics and teardown belong to their
  own gates - because a small, declared control surface is what R00 asks for, not a key per behaviour.

- Supersede the v80 alignment fix with a byte-wise copy as v81 (2026-11-08): `alignas(16)` did not change code generation — the rebuilt DLL still carried `movaps [rsp]` at the same `0x53f9c`, and a third crash (01:34, 0xC0000005, same offset) confirmed it. The faulting load reads the **source** on the stack while the ring-slot stores are `movups`, so declaring alignment only made the compiler more confident about `movaps`. v81 `f7382f59cd` (`2fcb029c…23b7b7`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `68d57d1e…`, CodeView age 420) copies records volatile byte-wise via `copy_probe_record` and drops the two 16-byte static asserts. **Offline proof**: scanning `.text` for the ring pattern now finds zero `movaps`/`movups` near it (previously 2 + 6). Suites 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v80 trio. Corrected anti-pattern: `alignas` is a promise to the compiler, not a repair — for frames entered through a hand-written thunk, either make the copy unvectorisable or fix the thunk and **verify by re-disassembly**, never by a passing static_assert. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v81-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Fix a probe-record alignment crash as v80 (2026-11-08): two panel-close crashes reported by WER (`SkyrimSE.exe` `0xC0000005`, faulting module `CharacterPanel.dll`, identical offset `0x53f9c`) disassemble to `movaps xmm0, [rsp]` in the 4096-entry ring copy. At 64 bytes per record the compiler vectorises the whole-record store with `movaps`, which needs 16-byte alignment, but `ProbeRecord`'s widest member is `uintptr_t` so it only had 8 and the static `ProbeState` carried that into the array. v79's two extra `uint16` counters made the record exactly 64 bytes — flipping `movups` to `movaps` and exposing the latent defect (v78's 60-byte record never triggered it). CrashLogger wrote no `crash-*.log`; WER was the reliable source. v80 `357f3f1644` (`8a830039…d7fb67`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `9ec7ec57…`, CodeView age 417) declares `alignas(16)` and pins both invariants with `static_assert`. Offline 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v79 trio. New anti-pattern: record layout changes flip the compiler's copy instructions, so alignment must be explicit and asserted for PODs that are bulk-copied inside static or heap containers. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v80-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).

- Close route A with a structural finding (2026-11-08 00:30, v77): the session (log 345,300 bytes `dc9bf10b…`, truncated before expiry so no close row) printed `ik=[mode:1 count:116 global:1 root:1]` on all 195 `e2a` rows. Static verification shows why: 32898 builds the update record with `mov byte [rsp+0x56],1` (ctx+0x2e) and 63602's tail with `mov dword [rsp+0x6b],0x1000101`, so `[g+0x249]` is a hard-coded 1 on both engine paths, and the engine-wide byte `0x2022530` has **no writer in the whole image** (three `cmp` reads only). The 58423 gate is therefore structurally always open once `count>0`, no posture can close it, and the `failed-no-ik == 0` criterion is vacuous — retired. The useful residue: 101 of 195 frames had `mix-failed=0` (v75: 93 of 249), a stable subset where the identity holds exactly, implying 58423 is called every frame but only rewrites bones when foot IK actually corrects. Next: **E2-A′** add a track→bone-name map and require every failing component to fall on foot-IK-affected bones; if residuals leave that set, escalate to **E2-B** and instrument 58423's input/output. - Implement the E2-A synthesised-pose identity as v72 (2026-11-06, **built and deployed, awaiting the run**): `probe_liveness_policy.h` gains the 64112 mix as pure functions — `probe_mix_trans` with the engine's `(1−w)·A + w·B` association, `probe_mix_rot` with the sign correction and zero-length convention, `probe_mix_delta` (absolute 2e-6, relative 1e-5 above 100 units), `probe_slot_view` (container → slot → tracks via the signed +0x34 offset behind the `dword[+4] > 2` gate) and `probe_compare_track` (translation channels 0–2, rotation 4–7) — plus a `ProbeMixTally` holding frames, tracks, failures, worst delta and four diagnostic samples. The existing 63856 entry recorder now reads `[g+0x230]`, both slot track arrays and poseLocal for the pinned graph, compares every track without sampling, and pushes one `k_mix_frame` record per frame; the render drain prints `e2a` rows and the close row gains `e2a-frames/tracks/failed/max-delta/ok/w-domain/non-finite/read-failed/samples`. No new detour, no native writes, no new INI key. Identity `CharacterPanel-scenegraph-v72-g5e2-a-mix-identity-5dd64a60d0-cl94faaed0c6-20261106T160252Z`, DLL 1,704,960 bytes SHA-256 `bb27a87f…538577`, PDB `7d809017…`, CodeView age 401; deployed with the concurrent INI after backing up the v71 trio. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v72-g5e2-a-2026-11-06).
- Pre-register [C-G5-E2](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2) E2-A, the synthesised-pose identity (2026-11-06, **documentation only — no implementation, no build, no run**): G5 requires comparing every contributing layer, weight, blend parameter, speed and final bone transform, so E2 is split into A (this contract), B (layer/weight enumeration via 63666/59303 and scale) and C (speed/clock ledger). Offline verification before the contract pinned the exact chain: 63582 takes `n = min(slot1.numData, slot0.numData, numAnim)` and calls `64112(count=n, A=slot1 tracks, B=slot0 tracks, out tracks, w)`, where `64112` mixes only `trans` and `rot` per 0x30-byte track — `trans = (1−w)·A + w·B`, `rot = normalize((1−w)·A + w·B′)` with `B′ = −B` when `dot<0` and an `rsqrtss`-plus-Newton normalisation (scale is not mixed; left for E2-B) — and the weight passed in is `[g+0x230]`, not 63581's internal `alpha`. The `[g+0x148]` overwrite lands in the payload while the memcpy source is the track array, so it cannot disturb poseLocal on the main path, and the fallback branch sets `[g+0x230] = 1.0f`, which makes the same identity degenerate to `slot0` without an exception. Observation needs **no new detour**: the existing 63856 entry hook already has `rcx=poseLocal`, `rdx=&g+0x160`, `r8d=count` and sees `[g+0x230]`, `[g+0x220]`, `[g+0x228]` before 63587 swaps slots. Criteria A1–A6 pre-register full-track coverage (no sampling), per-component absolute 2e-6 (relative 1e-5 above |v|>100), weight domain, and per-path reporting; stop conditions include non-finite values, `n` above capacity, invalid slot pointers, `w` outside [0,1] and any detail loss.

- Adjudicate the first v3 run and fix four instrument defects as v71 (2026-11-06): the v70 two-segment window (log 377,591 bytes `e07e71e8…`) installed all three hooks including the new return site and produced strong completion evidence — `apply-returns = apply-entries = 81,034` (every 63856 call returned through the hook) and `completions-pinned = pinned-apply-entries = 383` (the pinned source graph paired 383/383 on the same thread), with `pose-stable=yes`, `fill-le-cap=ok`, `skeleton=[cap:116 fill:116]` and zero transport gaps — but `apply-paths` was all-unclassified (81,034), hitting the R2 hard stop, so no criterion passes. Four code-side causes, all fixed: the classifier matched audit32's call-instruction addresses while the callee sees the return address (call+5, as `sample-entry caller=0xba3f6e` already showed); capacity/slot/pose facts were collected for every graph although the clone and others carry an uninitialized table (436 false `count-over-capacity`/`clamped-zero` and garbage slot words); returns from threads that never held a tracked entry were miscounted as orphans (80,651); and the in-flight high-water mark was sampled after the return. v71 `0327e15fa3` (`cbeba8a2…c722f4`, 1,633,792 bytes, PDB `16a29f59…`, CodeView age 400) adds per-path `apply-completions`, `untracked-returns`, and prints `path=`/`caller=` on every apply-entry row so an unclassified value is visible next time. Six+two+eight engine-free cases pass; built, not deployed. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e1-v3), [build](docs/build.md#v71-g5e1-v3-fix1-2026-11-06).
- Tell the bones that block from the bones that do not. The calibration already names up to eight
  bones nothing reproduced, but R03/R04 need to know which of them a draw actually reads, so the
  outliers are checked against the skin palette `NiSkinInstance::bones` points at: a bone a target
  mesh reads cannot be skipped, while a helper branch or a cloth chain the panel does not draw can.
  `SCOPY FP necessary … necessary=N helper=N palettes=bound/skinned` carries it, and `palettes=0/N` -
  a figure the engine has never rendered, runs 49-52's shape - reports the classification as
  `unbound-palette` rather than as "nothing is necessary", which is the difference between an answer
  and a silence.


- Take the mark twice in one frame, which is the only way to ask whether our own tick moved the
  graph's output. `FinalPoseProbe::sample_before_tick` runs before the drivers tick and records A0
  (both output buffers' identity, element counts and reasons, and the node locals), and the report
  after the tick adds an `A0A1` line per subject: the pose change this frame produced, the node
  locals it moved, and `nodes-rebound` for a tree that was rebuilt between the two samples. A
  cross-mark delta cannot separate our tick from the engine having run in between; in a paused world
  only one of the two can move anything, which is exactly what R02 asks. A3 is written out as
  `physics=skipped` so the six observation points are complete until the physics round and an absent
  stage cannot read as a passing one.


- Split the studio's pass batch away from who owns the figure it draws
  (`src/render/studio/figure_passes.{h,cpp}`): a request (root, accumulator, context, target, states,
  light slots, whether to read the bound target back once) and the batch itself - bind and clear the
  studio target, walk the figure's geometries, generate passes through the UI3D accumulator, submit
  each one with the studio rig swapped in and restored, then put the engine's ledger expectation back.
  The returned statistics describe the completed batch; retired S2 callbacks are removed. `CharacterClone::draw` now
  keeps only what is specific to a detached graph it owns - ownership gate, `pose()`, the animation
  base stage, the light anchor, the census - which is exactly the split the same-Actor route
  needs to draw an Actor's own third-person 3D through the same proven path. Two orderings moved
  deliberately and are commented where they moved: the DIRTY_RENDERTARGET reset now happens inside the
  batch (later is only more thorough, and nothing between the old and new points generates a pass or
  changes a binding), and the set now precedes the census (which never read the flag).

### Fixed

- Compile the default (Actor-route) configuration again. `CharacterClone`'s per-frame summary named
  `SceneGraphCopy::instance().animation_driver()` unconditionally, while that class only exists in the
  `CHARACTER_PANEL_SCENE_COPY_EXPERIMENT` configuration and its header include is gated by the same
  macro - so the default build had not compiled since that log line was added. A stale `build/`
  directory, configured before several source files existed, had been hiding it: the first real build
  in that configuration surfaced it. The line is now branched by configuration (the experiment keeps
  its exact line, the default logs the same summary without the driver name), and
  `cmake --preset Release` + `--target CharacterPanel` compiles and links.

- Complete the R03 evidence the next session has to produce, and measure R01's other prerequisite
  before anything is drawn. The comparison is now over whole transforms in both spaces: `order=` line
  carries `beyond`, `max-scale` (the third space, measured and never gating, because a pose carries
  three scales where a NIF node carries one) and `outliers=`, which names up to eight of the bones
  that missed the bar - R03 asks which ones, since a helper branch can be skipped while a target
  mesh's own bone cannot. A second `world` line composes the same pose along the animation skeleton's
  own parent table and compares it with the nodes' worlds: the local and world spaces are asked for
  separately because they disagree exactly where the two hierarchies do, which is what a CME/helper
  branch is. Both rules are pure header code and the unit test now pins them - a shifted order is
  convicted and named, an absent node leaves the comparison instead of shifting it, a scale
  difference never fails a bone, a world translation offset is reported as max-position rather than
  as a failure, and a world *rotation* disagreement is counted and named even when every local
  matched. A `geoms` line reports the Actor's own geometry inventory read-only: how many geometries,
  how many skinned, how many have a shader property (and how many of those are `EffectShader` by
  class), and how many already have device-side `rendererData` - the field runs 49-52 died on, which
  a figure the paused world never rendered simply does not have.

- Report AFP1's first in-game verdict and give its one blocker a lever
  ([verdict](docs/history-animation.md#final-output)). The pose track is readable and
  fully validated in game (`reason=ok`, 116 elements, 0x30 elements, data at
  `tracks+448`), and its index order is the **animation skeleton** bridged by
  exact bone name: 105 of 109 comparable bones land inside one degree, against
  45 of 106 for the `boneNodes` order - two arrays, two orders, since
  `hkbCharacter::poseLocal` measured as `boneNodes`-ordered in HKX2. The live
  buffer must be identified by content, not by index: the live one sat in slot 0
  in every mark, but the two buffer objects themselves moved between marks. The
  blocker is the parked puppet: its graph generates an output with a **live but
  empty** pose track (`num-data=0`, `numAnimBones=0`) while the player's carries
  116, and 2103 manual ticks moved two bones by 0.040 degrees - so the park
  (v6.25, chosen so the shell is invisible and untouchable) had moved it out of
  the engine's animation scope, and HKX42's "the manual tick does not propagate
  the pose to the nodes" now reads as "there is no output to propagate". The
  empty count gets its own `reason=pose-empty` instead of reading as a bad
  count, and NUMPAD1 cycles the placement between that park and standing at the
  player with the fade pinned (inside the scope), with `placement=` on the mark
  line - the one variable that isolates whether the emptiness is the park or the
  graph itself.

- Open the same-Actor route (PRD 0.9 section 6.6) with its first instrument, AFP1. That route draws
  the puppet Actor's own third-person 3D and applies the behaviour graph's final output pose to that
  Actor's own skeleton while the panel pauses the world, so `src/character/final_pose_probe.{h,cpp}`
  measures the two things it rests on, read-only. One mark (F11) walks the graph's own output buffers
  (`BShkbAnimationGraph::generatorOutputs[0]` and `[1]`): the output object, the tracks blob, the
  pose track's header, its element size and its data span are each validated in turn and the step
  that stops the read is named, so a buffer this build does not understand reports which assumption
  broke instead of printing zeros. It then keeps a copy of the pose as plain numbers (read with
  `memcpy`, because the offset and alignment are exactly what is being validated), prints what that
  copy did since the previous mark, prints what the engine-applied node locals did, and scores both
  candidate index orders - the engine's `boneNodes` table and the animation skeleton bridged by
  exact bone name - against those node locals, one line per candidate. The bar is the HKX28 one
  degree, fixed before the package ships. The player is sampled beside the puppet as the reference,
  because the engine drives that graph and those nodes itself. `PuppetDriver` gains the Actor
  reference, the manual tick's gate (F12, R02's push/freeze control, which keeps the clock fresh so
  unfreezing delivers one step rather than a catch-up) and its tick count. Nothing here writes a
  skeleton, detaches a graph, disables an Actor, resets a bind pose, resamples a clip or draws:
  AFP2 is where the measured order and buffer become an application and a draw.

- Make the same-Actor route's mapping decision a tested rule instead of a printed number, and read the
  camera chain the studio draw actually renders through. `score_pose_order` and `decide_pose_order`
  are inline header rules over plain data (the pose copy and a per-index `NodeLocal` table, whose
  `present` flag keeps an index without a node from being dropped and shifting the whole comparison):
  they score one index order against the node locals the engine applied and refuse to let it drive a
  skeleton unless at least 8 bones were comparable and 90% of them landed inside the HKX28 one-degree
  bar, with `no-candidate` / `insufficient-compared` / `below-ratio` named as the block. AFP2's write
  gate is that same call, not a second copy of it, and the unit test now convicts a shifted order,
  blocks a perfect score over four bones, and proves a ratio tie goes to the candidate that compared
  more bones. The mark also reports the camera chain: `NiAccumulator::camera` (the camera the
  accumulator generating the studio's passes was started with) against `ui3d->camera` and the world
  camera, the camera's view axis and projection frustum, and where the subject's own 3D root sits -
  world position, distance along the view axis, distance off it, fade and bound. The pass submission
  takes no camera and writes no projection, so that identity is what decides whether the route that
  may not translate the Actor per frame adapts the camera instead; it is read, not moved to find out.

### Fixed

- Follow the clip search's own rules when scanning a node: an object is scanned as far as it is
  readable instead of only when a whole 0x200-byte window is, which is what had been skipping every
  0x78-byte `StateInfo` - the node a state's generator hangs from - so the walk burned its object
  budget on the state machine's incidental pointers and reported no clip at all. The walk also asks
  the engine for each object's class name now (only on objects whose first word already looks like a
  vtable) and takes `hkbClipGenerator` as the answer, rather than inferring it from the animation
  name; depth and object limits are wider, and two histograms (`classes=`, `rejections=`) plus a
  `capped=` flag say whether a future miss is a lost walk or an absent clip.

R1 stays diagnostic-only; no candidate hash repetition substitutes for a source oracle. - Implement the E2-B foot-IK observation as v83 (2026-11-08): two new detours on 58423 — entry (7-byte chain-addressing head) and return (the single `ret`'s tail window, both verified offline for interior jump targets). The entry recorder snapshots the out block through the policy reader `probe_read_footik_out` and registers a per-tid in-flight entry; the return recorder re-reads, compares component-wise with the E2-A thresholds and pushes `b-entry`/`b-return` pairs. Since the out block is what 63581 memcpy-s into poseLocal, `entry != return` components are foot-IK rewrites and the poseLocal consistency checks are judged without hooking the copy sites. Close row gains `b-calls/b-compared/b-rewritten/b-max-delta/b-clean/b-overwritten/b-entries/b-returns/b-last-rewritten`; pool 3→5. Offline e2b 3 + e2a 13 + v3 6 + repair 2 + legacy 8 = 42 pass. [Contract](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2-b), [build](docs/build.md#v83-g5e2-b-2026-11-08).
- Converge E2-A: every residual lands on one of three enumerable post-process chains (2026-11-08, v82 run, log 404,515 bytes `c6447fe0…`): 436 fallback frames, `e2a-failed=10,421` of 354,032 components (2.94%), `fail-frames=312/436`, `fail-foot=5,921`, `fail-cloth=626`, `fail-unexplained=3,874` — and the eight aggregated bone slots are all **arm chain and attachment** (`NPC L/R Forearm`, `R UpperArm`, `L/R Hand`, `AnimObjectL`, `Shield`, `L Pauldron`), i.e. hand IK plus equipment attachment, a third post-process class beside foot IK and cloth. Sample deltas are 3.2e-6..7.6e-6 (relative 1e-5..5e-5), above plain lerp rounding, so by the pre-registered bounds they are genuine failures and were **not** relaxed away. A separate threshold-argument fix (v83, committed but **not built or deployed**) changes the rule to "absolute OR relative, whichever is looser" because relative error is scale-free; it does not absorb those samples, so the reading stands. **E2-A conclusion**: the identity is exact off the post-process chains (148/223 frames component-wise equal in v81) but a strict zero-residual identity is unreachable by design, since 63581's memcpy consumes an `out` block already processed by 58423 (foot IK), with hand IK and attachment rewriting bones as well; the criterion should become "residuals ⊆ enumerable post-process bone set". Next: E2-B, hooking 58423 (and locating the hand-IK/attachment entries) to observe post-process input/output explicitly. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2).

Every "other" residual was therefore a **cloth** bone, not an unexplained one, so the foot-IK-only split was too narrow and the fixed sample table hid the real set. v82 `36cc25bbca` (`229547fe…18505dd`, 1,780,224 bytes, PDB `45c7be66…`, CodeView age 422) classifies foot/cloth/unexplained, aggregates unexplained names by bone with counts (up to 8), and replaces `e2a-fail-other` with `e2a-fail-cloth`/`e2a-fail-unexplained`. - Supersede the v80 alignment fix with a byte-wise copy as v81 (2026-11-08): `alignas(16)` did not change code generation — the rebuilt DLL still carried `movaps [rsp]` at the same `0x53f9c`, and a third crash (01:34, 0xC0000005, same offset) confirmed it. The faulting load reads the **source** on the stack while the ring-slot stores are `movups`, so declaring alignment only made the compiler more confident about `movaps`. v81 `f7382f59cd` (`2fcb029c…23b7b7`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `68d57d1e…`, CodeView age 420) copies records volatile byte-wise via `copy_probe_record` and drops the two 16-byte static asserts. **Offline proof**: scanning `.text` for the ring pattern now finds zero `movaps`/`movups` near it (previously 2 + 6). Suites 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v80 trio. Corrected anti-pattern: `alignas` is a promise to the compiler, not a repair — for frames entered through a hand-written thunk, either make the copy unvectorisable or fix the thunk and **verify by re-disassembly**, never by a passing static_assert. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v81-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).
- Fix a probe-record alignment crash as v80 (2026-11-08): two panel-close crashes reported by WER (`SkyrimSE.exe` `0xC0000005`, faulting module `CharacterPanel.dll`, identical offset `0x53f9c`) disassemble to `movaps xmm0, [rsp]` in the 4096-entry ring copy. At 64 bytes per record the compiler vectorises the whole-record store with `movaps`, which needs 16-byte alignment, but `ProbeRecord`'s widest member is `uintptr_t` so it only had 8 and the static `ProbeState` carried that into the array. v79's two extra `uint16` counters made the record exactly 64 bytes — flipping `movups` to `movaps` and exposing the latent defect (v78's 60-byte record never triggered it). CrashLogger wrote no `crash-*.log`; WER was the reliable source. v80 `357f3f1644` (`8a830039…d7fb67`, 1,779,200 bytes, PDB `9ec7ec57…`, CodeView age 417) declares `alignas(16)` and pins both invariants with `static_assert`. Offline 12/6/2/8 pass; deployed after backing up the v79 trio. New anti-pattern: record layout changes flip the compiler's copy instructions, so alignment must be explicit and asserted for PODs that are bulk-copied inside static or heap containers. [Reading](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2), [build](docs/build.md#v80-g5e2-ap-2026-11-08).

- Close route A with a structural finding (2026-11-08 00:30, v77): the session (log 345,300 bytes `dc9bf10b…`, truncated before expiry so no close row) printed `ik=[mode:1 count:116 global:1 root:1]` on all 195 `e2a` rows. - Pre-register [C-G5-E2](docs/independent-behavior-graph.md#c-g5-e2) E2-A, the synthesised-pose identity (2026-11-06, **documentation only — no implementation, no build, no run**): G5 requires comparing every contributing layer, weight, blend parameter, speed and final bone transform, so E2 is split into A (this contract), B (layer/weight enumeration via 63666/59303 and scale) and C (speed/clock ledger). Offline verification before the contract pinned the exact chain: 63582 takes `n = min(slot1.numData, slot0.numData, numAnim)` and calls `64112(count=n, A=slot1 tracks, B=slot0 tracks, out tracks, w)`, where `64112` mixes only `trans` and `rot` per 0x30-byte track — `trans = (1−w)·A + w·B`, `rot = normalize((1−w)·A + w·B′)` with `B′ = −B` when `dot<0` and an `rsqrtss`-plus-Newton normalisation (scale is not mixed; left for E2-B) — and the weight passed in is `[g+0x230]`, not 63581's internal `alpha`. The `[g+0x148]` overwrite lands in the payload while the memcpy source is the track array, so it cannot disturb poseLocal on the main path, and the fallback branch sets `[g+0x230] = 1.0f`, which makes the same identity degenerate to `slot0` without an exception. Observation needs **no new detour**: the existing 63856 entry hook already has `rcx=poseLocal`, `rdx=&g+0x160`, `r8d=count` and sees `[g+0x230]`, `[g+0x220]`, `[g+0x228]` before 63587 swaps slots. Criteria A1–A6 pre-register full-track coverage (no sampling), per-component absolute 2e-6 (relative 1e-5 above |v|>100), weight domain, and per-path reporting; stop conditions include non-finite values, `n` above capacity, invalid slot pointers, `w` outside [0,1] and any detail loss.
